# AI Handover Note: Developer Guide

Setup map, port directory, command recipes, and build history for the next AI agent or developer picking up this codebase.

---

## 1. System Environment & Port Bindings

- **Go**: Portable Go 1.22.5 at `$env:USERPROFILE\go-portable\go`.
- **PostgreSQL**: Portable 16.3 at `$env:USERPROFILE\pg-portable\pgsql`.
  - Port: **5435** (trust auth, no password)
  - Data dir: `$env:USERPROFILE\pg-data`
  - User/DB: `postgres` / `custom_erp`
- **Web app**: port **8080** (`http://localhost:8080`).
- **Login required** (since 2026-07-12). Dev credentials: `DEV_CREDENTIALS.local.txt` (project root, gitignored).
- **Start/stop everything**: `.\manage.ps1` (menu) or `start`/`stop`/`restart`/`status`/`logs`.
  - Known issue: can hang in sandboxed/background shells — see §3.C.

## 3. Development Command Recipes

Run all commands from the repo root: `c:\Users\ABCD\Documents\Antigravity Projects\ERP`

**A. Unit tests**
```powershell
& "$env:USERPROFILE\go-portable\go\bin\go.exe" test ./...
```

**B. Compile backend**
- Dev build (keeps debug symbols):
  ```powershell
  & "$env:USERPROFILE\go-portable\go\bin\go.exe" build -o erp-server.exe ./cmd/server
  ```
- Release build (stripped, ~30% smaller): `.\manage.ps1 release`, or:
  ```powershell
  & "$env:USERPROFILE\go-portable\go\bin\go.exe" build -ldflags="-s -w" -o erp-server.exe ./cmd/server
  ```
- Always target `./cmd/server` — the repo root is no longer package `main` (since Stage 19).
- Windows locks a running `.exe`; `manage.ps1 release` stops the server first but does not restart it.

**C. Start PostgreSQL**
```powershell
& "$env:USERPROFILE\pg-portable\pgsql\bin\postgres.exe" -D "$env:USERPROFILE\pg-data" -p 5435
```
- Run as a foreground process in sandboxed shells — `pg_ctl start` (detached) gets killed with the session.
- Known issue: `manage.ps1`'s `pg_ctl start -w` can hang in a backgrounded/sandboxed shell even though Postgres itself started fine. Check `pg_isready -p 5435` directly if `manage.ps1 start` looks stuck.

**D. Start the server**
```powershell
& "c:\Users\ABCD\Documents\Antigravity Projects\ERP\erp-server.exe"
```

---

## 4. Multi-Tenant Development Standards

When building any new module:
1. **Resolve tenant** — read the `Resolved-Tenant-ID` header in every handler.
2. **Scope every query** — call `db.SetSearchPath(tx, schema)` before any query. Never hardcode a schema.
3. **Audit everything** — log every document transition, submission, and approval.
4. **Use the numbering engine** — `engines.GenerateSequence()` / `GenerateVariantCode()`, never a hand-rolled counter.

---

## 5. Omnichannel Scale Architecture

Design rules for scaling 1 store → 2,000 stores without locking the transaction DB:

1. **Outbox pattern for external sync** — never call Shopify/payment/GST APIs synchronously from a user-facing transaction.
   - Write the record + an outbox event in the same SQL transaction.
   - A background worker publishes the event asynchronously.
2. **Available-to-Sell (ATS)** — never query the raw ledger for real-time channels. Use:
   `ATS = Available - Reserved - Safety Stock - Channel Holds`
3. **Idempotency** — every webhook payload must carry a signature, timestamp, and unique ID to block duplicate orders/invoices.

    - **A real bug caught mid-build**: `StartJobRunnerRetentionSweeper`'s first draft passed a schema name straight into `SweepJobRunnerRetention(tenantID string)`, which resolves via `db.GetTenantSchema` internally - `GetTenantSchema("tenant_acme")` does not resolve (tenant ids look like `"acme"`, not `"tenant_acme"`) and silently falls back to `tenant_default`, so every non-default tenant's hourly sweep would have quietly swept `tenant_default`'s jobs instead of its own. Fixed via the existing `tenantIDForSchema` helper - the exact fix `StartPublicAPIRuntimeSweeper` (Stage 38.3/5/9) already needed for the identical reason. **If you write a new schema-fanout worker that calls a tenantID-based function, resolve `tenantIDForSchema(schema)` first - do not pass the schema name where a tenantID is expected, `GetTenantSchema` will not error, it will just silently resolve to the wrong tenant.**
  - **38.4 (built second, rides 38.6's runner)**: `WebhookSubscription` (plain generic-doc-API doctype, the `ScheduledReport` precedent) + `engines/webhook.go` (+`_test.go`): `dispatchWebhooksForEvent` is called from `processOutbox` (`engines/outbox.go`) once an event is already committed, fanning out to one `webhook_delivery` async job per matching Active subscription - the job runner's own retry/backoff/DeadLettered **is** this item's DLQ, no second mechanism. Real HMAC-SHA256 signing (`X-Webhook-Signature`, mandatory secret). New module_key `integrations` registered proactively in the same migration (Stage 37.9's lesson applied without needing to be re-learned live).
    - **The SSRF guard is the one piece worth re-reading if you touch webhook delivery again**: `validateWebhookURL` (https-only, every resolved IP must be genuinely public - no loopback/private/link-local/unspecified/multicast) is called **twice** - at document-create time (convenience) and again immediately before every delivery attempt (the real guarantee, since DNS can change between the two - a create-time-only check is a classic TOCTOU SSRF bypass). Redirects are never followed (`http.ErrUseLastResponse`) for the same reason. Stdlib `net`/`net/http` only, no new dependency.
    - **Live-verified against a TEST-NET-2 address (198.51.100.1, RFC 5737), never a real third party**: seeded a real outbox event, watched the live `processOutbox`/job-runner tickers actually enqueue and attempt a genuine HTTPS POST, which correctly timed out (`context deadline exceeded`) and rescheduled - proving the full live pipeline end-to-end, something the unit tests (which call the pieces directly) cannot prove on their own.

  - **Learned from Stage 37.9's module-registration bug and checked proactively this time**: `ReorderPointConfig.module_key = 'inventory'`, and `inventory` is confirmed `is_core = true` in `public.modules` (checked via direct SQL before writing a single line of migration) - so this stage does NOT need the `public.modules`/`module_entitlements` registration Stage 37.9 had to retroactively add for `quality`/`service`. **If you add a doctype with a module_key that isn't one of core/master_data/inventory/sales/finance/wms/oms, check `SELECT is_core FROM public.modules WHERE module_key = '...'` before assuming the generic doc API will accept it** - an `is_core=false` module_key needs its own `public.modules`+`module_entitlements` registration in the same migration, or every doctype under it will silently 403 with `SAAS-0191` on the generic create path (dedicated, non-`moduleGate` routes are unaffected, which is why this class of bug hides from a live check that only exercises dedicated routes).
  - **`CalculateWeightedVelocity`'s one real approximation, stated rather than hidden**: `CalculateSalesVelocity` always looks back from TODAY - there is no "days N to M ago" query anywhere in this codebase to build a true isolated prior-period velocity from. So the "prior" window this function blends against is actually the velocity over the WHOLE `recentDays+priorDays` window, not a genuinely separate earlier period. It still weights recent activity more heavily than a flat single-window average (which is the actual improvement being claimed), but it is not textbook exponential smoothing or a true two-period trend comparison - the doc comment says this explicitly so a future reader doesn't assume more precision than the function delivers.

  - **The mirrored-posting design, if you touch this again**: an `IntercompanyTransaction`'s two legs are deliberately TWO separate `PostDoubleEntry` calls (FROM entity Dr 1700/Cr its account; TO entity Dr its account/Cr 2500), not one combined call, because `PostingOptions.Entity` is a whole-posting dimension and the two legs need two different entities. This makes the pair non-atomic by construction — a mid-transaction failure (e.g. a closed period on one side) lands the document in "Partially Posted" rather than silently dropping the second leg, and `RetryPostApprovedIntercompanyTransaction` is the only way forward from there (mirrors `RetryPostApprovedJournalVoucher` exactly, including refusing to retry an already-`Posted` document). **Do not try to "fix" this into one atomic transaction without also restructuring `PostingOptions` to carry a per-line entity map** — that's a much bigger change than this stage's scope justified.

Fixed by giving `entityControlAccountBalance` an explicit `normalSide` ("debit"/"credit") parameter. **If you add a third control account to this pattern, get its normal side right the first time** — this is the second time in this codebase's history a signed-balance mixup has shipped in a first draft (37.1.5's presentation trial balance had a related class of issue).

  - **A real gap found and closed, the kind worth re-checking if you add a new PIM bulk path**: `RejectRestrictedFieldWrites` (field-level write permissions, Stage 16.7) was only ever wired into the generic single-document update handler. `BulkUpdateDocuments` (PIM bulk-edit/Group Actions) and the whole CSV/template import path (`BulkImportCSV`→`runDocDataImport`→`importBatch`, and `RunPIMImportTemplate`) never called it, so the shipped Cashier/`Item.gst_rate` policy could be bypassed in bulk. Fixed by adding the check once inside `BulkUpdateDocuments` itself (before the field is applied to any row) and once inside `importBatch`'s per-row loop (skipped, by design, for the two system-initiated import paths — the webhook hook and the scheduled drop-directory worker — which pass `role=""` since there's no operator session to check against). **If you add a fourth way to write PIM documents in bulk, it needs this same call — it is not automatic just by going through `ValidateDocument`.**
  - **The GS1 barcode check-digit algorithm is numbered right-to-left, not left-to-right — do not "simplify" it.** `gs1CheckDigit` (`engines/pim_barcode.go`) numbers digit positions from the digit immediately left of the check digit outward; a naive left-to-right numbering happens to give the same answer for a 12-digit base (EAN-13/UPC-A) but the **wrong** answer for a 7-digit base (EAN-8), because flipping the numbering direction only preserves odd/even parity when the base length is even. Pinned against three published real-world GTINs in `pim_barcode_test.go`, not hand-computed fixtures, specifically so a re-implementation can't silently reintroduce this. `GenerateEANBarcode` seeds from GS1's own `020`-`029` restricted-circulation range via a dedicated `PIMBarcodeSeq` numbering series (empty prefix/separator, `NEVER` reset — see the migration) — it is not a licensed public GTIN generator and the code says so.

  - **A real pre-existing bug was found and fixed: `db.InitDB` never closed the previous connection pool before opening a new one.** Harmless in production (called once at startup), but the test suite calls it at the top of nearly every DB-touching test function in a package, and by this session enough test files existed that `go test ./engines/...` deterministically exhausted Postgres's 100-connection limit partway through the run (reproduced 3 times in a row, always at the same test, before the cause was found). Fixed with one `if DB != nil { DB.Close() }` at the top of `InitDB` in `db/db.go` — three lines, benefits every existing and future test in the suite, not just this session's new ones. **If a future `go test ./... -p 1` run ever reports "too many clients already" again, this fix is the first thing to check hasn't been reverted/lost**, not a reason to assume the suite is newly flaky.
  - **The design, if you touch either stage again**: `ApplyPIMTransformRule` (`pim_transform.go`) is the *only* seam that runs a rule's steps — both `BuildChannelPayload` (export, via `ChannelFieldMap.transform_rule`) and `RunPIMImportTemplate` (import, via a `PIMImportTemplate` column mapping) call it, so the two can never disagree about what a rule does to a value. `RunPIMImportTemplate` does not reimplement CSV validation/batching — it remaps a source file's columns into the same fieldname→value shape `BulkImportCSV` always produced, then calls the identical shared core (`runDocDataImport`). **If you need a third way to feed rows into an Item import (bulk API endpoint, a different file format), build it as a third producer of that same `[]map[string]interface{}` + parallel `preErrors []string` shape and call `runDocDataImport` — do not write a fourth copy of the batching/dry-run/create-update-classification logic.**

  - **The GL design, if you need to touch settlement reconciliation again**: one shared poster, `postSettlementLineGL`, used by both the auto-match path and the write-off path — never two GL-writing code paths for what is accounting-identical. It credits Accounts Receivable (1300) for the **invoiced** value (not the marketplace's reported gross), debits Cash/Bank/Commission/Fees/TDS/TCS for what the settlement line itself reports, and posts whatever gap remains to the new Settlement Variance Written Off (5270) account — debit if we were short-changed vs our own invoice, credit if the marketplace reported more than we billed. This is what makes the entry balance in both directions without a special case; don't special-case the "variance = 0" (clean match) case into a different code path than write-off, they're the same formula with a smaller final term.
  - **No Item-master cost/price field exists anywhere in this codebase** (same gap `resolveSalesOrderLinePrices`'s own comment in `returns.go` already documented for RTO cost) — this is why 35.8's variance is computed against the *invoice*, not an independently-priced expectation, and why 35.9's exchange feature is deliberately same-value-only (it reuses the returned line's own cost basis for the exchange leg rather than trying to look one up that isn't there). If a real Item cost/price master ever gets built, both of these become candidates to tighten.
  - **Reverse pickup reuses the Stage 26.12.4/35.5 courier machinery completely unmodified** — `CreateLogisticsBooking`, `AllocateCourierAWB`, `ScheduleCourierPickup` are called exactly as a forward shipment calls them; only a `shipment_direction=Reverse` tag plus a `return_request_id` link (patched onto the booking after creation) and one new branch in `RecordDeliveryEvent` distinguish it. If you add a third shipment direction later, extend that one branch rather than adding a parallel tracking-ingestion path.
  - **A real, pre-existing gap was found and stated, not fixed here**: the `ReturnRequest`/`RefundRequest` workflow (Stage 26.12.5 — request → approve → receive → QC → refund) has **never had a management UI**. - **2026-08-22 — Stage 35.7 (bundles/kits/virtual SKUs) BUILT. CODE + UI + 1 NEW MIGRATION (applied + ledger-recorded on dev only), UNCOMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED.** New: `db/migrations_stage35_7_bundles_kits.sql`, `engines/bundles.go`, `engines/bundles_stage35_test.go`, `internal/server/handlers_bundles.go`. Narrow edits: bundle expansion/attribution in `orders.go`, derived availability in `inventory.go` and `channel_sync.go`, ProductBundle generic-save validation, two routes and one OMS panel. No dependency.
  - **Do not merge ProductBundle with manufacturing BOM/VAS by name alone.** ProductBundle is commercial: Virtual derives ATS and explodes for picking; Stocked is assembled stock. Manufacturing BOM remains the production recipe with its own lifecycle/QC/snapshot rules. Nesting ProductBundles is refused, avoiding cycles and ambiguous commercial pricing.
  - **Virtual ATS is conservative by facility.** Every component calls `computeATS`; one location's bundle count is the minimum component ratio, and the network count sums those complete-location counts. Do not change it to `min(sum(component stock))` unless split-component fulfillment becomes mandatory everywhere—that formula advertises a bundle whose parts may be stranded at separate facilities.
  - **Explosion occurs before validation/sourcing/reservation.** Component SalesOrderLines carry `bundle_sku`, `bundle_quantity`, `bundle_component_qty`, `bundle_pricing_mode`; the order header carries `bundle_lines`. Parent/fixed prices are distributed, final component takes the remainder. Stocked kits never explode.
  - **Assembly is genuinely atomic and retry-safe.** `PostBundleAssembly` owns one transaction containing the operation evidence, ATS-guarded inventory mutations and StockLedgerEntry rows. Optional `idempotency_key` returns the prior Completed operation. A failed shortage leaves no `BundleAssembly` row. Keep this stronger boundary; do not replace it with sequential calls to `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucher`, whose separate operation-document update would leave a retry ambiguity after a post-commit error.
  - **Migration/verification**: surgically applied to dev and propagated to both current tenant schemas; ledger contains 35.7 and still does not contain the parallel `migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql`. `TestStage357BundlesKitsAndVirtualSKUs` passes, and final `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js`, `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` are fully green. Nothing staged/committed.

- **2026-08-22 — Stage 42.6 (labour and billing depth) BUILT. CODE + 1 NEW MIGRATION, UNCOMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED.** User explicitly instructed the full phase despite its low-priority/3PL gate; all **nine** listed rows were built (the plan heading's “8 items” is a pre-existing counting mismatch). New files: `db/migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql`, `engines/wms_labour_billing.go`, `engines/wms_stage42_6_test.go`, `internal/server/handlers_wms_labour_billing.go`. Narrow edits: `engines/warehouse_task.go` completes an owner-attributed task → captured-charge event hook; `internal/server/routes.go` has seven WMS labour/billing routes. No dependencies and no dedicated frontend; configured masters are reachable through the generic DocType UI and reports through the existing catalog.
  - **The labour standard is deliberately structural.** A `LaborStandard` references comma-separated active element/allowance/travel master codes; `ResolveLaborStandardTime` recomputes time, including per-unit/fixed travel and percentage/fixed allowances. There is no stored total to drift. `GetLaborPlan` reads open WarehouseTasks and returns `uncovered_tasks` when a tenant has not configured an operation/standard — it must not fabricate a staffing target.
  - **Billing's durable boundary is `CapturedCharge`, not a report calculation.** Completed tasks auto-capture only when they resolve an owner from their bin and match configured owner/location/task-type charge rules. The capture id derives from event key + charge code, so retrying cannot duplicate a charge. Errors are logged but do not undo a physical task completion. `ChargeContract` terms (date/owner/rate group) layer on top of code/group markup, discount, minimum and tax.
  - **`GenerateInvoiceFromCapturedCharges` posts a real existing SalesInvoice.** The owner/date batch id is deterministic. A prior Draft is retried/postable; a successful existing invoice merely links its still-Captured evidence. Do not replace this with a fresh invoice id on retry, or a temporary GL error will duplicate an invoice. Storage v2 works from `StorageBalanceSnapshot` day rows only, exposes `snapshot_days`, and does not fall back to current `bin_stock` while claiming historical accuracy.
  - **Verification status:** focused `go test ./engines -run '^TestStage42_6LabourBillingDepth$' -count=1 -v` passed (engineered standard → plan → automatic task event → daily snapshot/storage charge → Posted SalesInvoice); `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, and the `internal/server` compile test pass. **Do not run `erp-server -migrate` blindly:** the shared tree also has uncommitted Stage 42.5/courier migrations, so it would apply work outside this phase. Stage 42.6's migration has not been applied to dev or production.

- **2026-08-22 — Stage 35.5 (real courier integration) BUILT. CODE + 1 NEW MIGRATION (applied + ledger-recorded on dev only), UNCOMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED.** Built all six items using D2's documented default providers (Delhivery + Shiprocket). New files: `engines/courier.go` (adapter contract, encrypted-credential bridge, AWB/pickup/cancel/rates/webhook/NDR orchestration), `engines/courier_adapters.go`, `engines/shipping_label_pdf.go`, `engines/courier_stage35_test.go`, `internal/server/handlers_courier.go`, `internal/server/handlers_courier_test.go`, `db/migrations_stage35_5_courier_integration.sql`. Modified: one additive block in `internal/server/routes.go`, two exact public-webhook entries + rate category in `middleware.go`, plus the three trackers/brain map. Zero new dependencies, no frontend change. What the next session needs to know:
  - **Credentials remain the honest live boundary.** Delhivery expects `api_token`, `pickup_name`, `webhook_secret`; Shiprocket accepts `access_token` or `email`/`password`, plus `webhook_secret` (and optionally `courier_id`). They are AES-256-GCM encrypted in the existing `channel_credentials` table under `courier:<provider>` and never returned. No live provider request was claimed without real accounts.
  - **Provider AWB allocation is deliberately a second, explicit step after `CreateLogisticsBooking`.** The existing local AWB keeps legacy/manual flows usable; `AllocateCourierAWB` replaces it only after provider success and records `provider_awb`. A retry returns that AWB idempotently. Do not move the remote call inside the database insert without adding an outbox — a network timeout inside that path would make it impossible to know whether the courier bought one AWB or two.
  - **The PDF has a real Code 128-B, not barcode-looking decoration.** It calculates the ISO/IEC 15417 checksum and draws each module as a PDF vector rectangle. It calls `GenerateShippingLabel` first, so 35.4.3's invoice-before-label refusal and `label_generated_at` stamp remain one shared rule. QZ/ZPL remains the silent-print path.
  - **Tracking security and idempotency**: inbound payloads require `X-Courier-Signature` = hex HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body using the encrypted `webhook_secret`. `CourierTrackingEvent` has a provider+event-id unique partial index; Delhivery/Shiprocket payloads normalize into `RecordDeliveryEvent`, `RecordNDR` or `RecordRTO`. NDR is its own `NDRCase` lifecycle (Open/Reattempt Scheduled/Closed/RTO), not an invented LogisticsBooking status.
  - **The two webhook paths are intentionally public and narrowly enumerated** in `publicRoutes`; a courier cannot present a human bearer token. The handler still fails closed on missing/invalid HMAC, and both paths use the 30/min webhook rate budget. An arbitrary `{provider}` path is not public; the regression test pins that boundary.
  - **Verification**: `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` clean on the combined tree after concurrent Stage 42.6's handler settled; migration-backed Stage 35.5 tests green; `internal/server`, `internal/kb` and `db` suites green. The attempted whole `go test ./... -p 1` run hit the already-documented `db.InitDB` pool leak (`too many clients`) in unrelated engine tests; the Stage 35 suite was rerun clean afterward. Brain redraw/check is 100% coverage, 0 unclaimed files.

  - **A real, pre-existing gap was found and fixed while building the freeze: picking allocation never respected `bin_status` at all.** `PutawayToBin` and the directed-putaway candidate query have refused a `Blocked`/`Full`/`Counting` bin since 42.2.6 — but `allocateByOrder`/`allocateFEFO` (`engines/traceability.go`, what `AllocateFromStock` actually calls for both picking and wave picking) never filtered on it, so a bin marked `Blocked` could still be picked from. Fixed by adding the same `NOT IN ('Blocked', 'Full', 'Counting')` filter to both queries' bin join. **This is a real behavior change to existing picking, not scoped narrowly to physical-inventory bins** — any bin a tenant has already marked `Blocked`/`Full` for an unrelated reason will now also be excluded from picking, which is almost certainly what `Blocked`/`Full` were supposed to mean all along, but confirm this reads correctly against any live tenant that has bins in those states before deploying.
  - **`binCapacityHeadroom` (the new read-only sibling of `enforceBinCapacity`, used by slotting v2's dimension-aware re-slot and consolidation) initially did not tolerate a missing `Item` row the way `enforceBinCapacity` itself does** (`err != nil && err != sql.ErrNoRows`) — caught by the new test suite itself (`SlottingV2UnslottingAndConsolidation` failed until fixed), not live. Fixed to match `enforceBinCapacity`'s exact tolerance. **If you write a third capacity-check function, copy `enforceBinCapacity`'s `sql.ErrNoRows` handling on all three of its queries — a SKU with no Item master row is a normal case here (bin_stock/velocity data can exist without every SKU also having a full Item master), not an error.**
  - **`GetUnslottingSuggestions` keys off `Item.status = 'Cancelled'`, not an `Inactive` value — this codebase's Item doctype has no separate Active/Inactive lifecycle field at all.** Confirmed by grepping every existing Item-status check in the codebase (`engines/pim.go`'s public API filters, etc.) before picking the signal — all of them already treat `Cancelled` as "not a live item." Don't add a new `discontinued`/`Inactive` flag to Item for this without a real product reason; the signal that already exists was sufficient.

New files: `db/migrations_stage45_money_paise.sql`. Modified (~20 files): `engines/finance.go` (new `RupeesToPaise`/`PaiseToRupees`/`PaiseMap` helpers, `PostDoubleEntry`/`GetTrialBalance` retyped to paise), `engines/pos_checkout.go`, `engines/gst.go`-adjacent postings in `finance.go`, `engines/vendor_invoice.go`, `engines/sales_invoice.go`, `engines/currency_fx.go`, `engines/tds.go`, `engines/notes.go`, `engines/returns.go`, `engines/fulfillment.go`, `engines/hr_payroll.go`, `engines/assets.go`, `engines/marketplace.go`, `engines/journal_voucher.go`, `engines/bank_reconciliation.go`, `engines/reports.go`, `engines/finance_reports_stage26.go`, `engines/gl_cost_center.go`, `engines/report_definitions.go`, `engines/master_data_validation.go` (Item code charset). **No new dependency.** What the next session needs to know:
  - **⚠️ ONE NEW MIGRATION, AND THE `//go:embed *.sql` TRAP APPLIES — BUT THIS ONE IS NOT LIKE THE OTHERS: IT MUTATES EXISTING DATA, NOT JUST SCHEMA.** `migrations_stage45_money_paise.sql` does `ALTER COLUMN ... TYPE BIGINT` then `UPDATE ... SET debit = debit * 100, credit = credit * 100` on `gl_postings`, across `tenant_default` and every `tenant_*` schema. On a virgin DB this only exercises the ALTER path (nothing to backfill). On dev/prod it will **multiply every existing gl_postings row's debit/credit by 100 in place** — correct and intentional (existing rows were whole rupees; the column now means paise), but it is the one step in this whole stage that is not simply additive. **Do not run `-migrate` against dev or prod without a fresh backup first**, and ship the new binary in the same window as the migration — code between the two that still assumes whole-rupee `int` would misread the column.
  - **Filename says "stage45", the checklist entry is Stage 46 — this is intentional, not a mistake to fix.** Discovered mid-session that a concurrent session had already taken "Stage 45" for an unrelated UI/UX sweep (see the entry a few down). Renaming the migration file after the fact was rejected: migration filenames are permanent once a database's `public.schema_migrations` ledger might reference them, and no database has applied this one outside a throwaway scratch instance yet, so there's no live collision — but don't create a `migrations_stage46_*.sql` file later and be surprised the numbering doesn't match the checklist Stage number.
  - **Architecture, if you need to touch money code again**: paise internal, rupees external. `gl_postings` and `PostDoubleEntry`'s `debits`/`credits` maps are `int64` paise. Every report/API boundary converts back to rupees via `PaiseToRupees` before it leaves the function — `GetTrialBalance`, `GetProfitAndLoss`, `GetBalanceSheet`, `GetCashFlowStatement`, `GetTaxLedgerReport`, `GetStatutoryGLExport`, `getGLAccountBook`, `GetCostCenterPL`, `glAccountNetBalance` (GST return filing depends on this one), `GetFXGainLossRegister`, `GetTrialBalanceInPresentationCurrency`, and `ReconcileBankStatement`'s bank-statement matcher. **If you add a new function that reads `gl_postings.debit`/`.credit` directly, it is paise — convert with `PaiseToRupees` before returning or comparing against anything rupee-denominated (a bank statement amount, a user-typed figure, another report).** Grepping `gl_postings` in the whole tree is how this session found every site that needed it, including two (`bank_reconciliation.go`, `report_definitions.go`'s `getGLAccountBook`) the initial scope survey missed.
  - **Two different fix strategies were used depending on whether a genuine float source sits close to the posting — know which is which before extending either.** Where the money value already exists as a precise `float64` right next to where it gets truncated (POS cart line totals, GST component split, debit/credit notes, TDS payment, refund processing) — the fix moved the conversion from `int(x)` to `RupeesToPaise(x)` **at that float**, which is what actually restores precision, not just widens storage. Where a subsystem is deliberately whole-rupee-only with no nearby float — FX revaluation/settlement (its cumulative unrealised balance is cross-period **persisted state**, re-deriving its precision was judged higher-risk than this migration's scope), payroll, vendor-invoice payment, journal vouchers, marketplace settlement, asset capitalisation — the existing rupee map is scaled mechanically via the new `PaiseMap()` helper right before `PostDoubleEntry`. **Do not assume every `PaiseMap()` call site is "not yet fixed properly" — some are a deliberate scope boundary, documented inline at each site.**
  - **The single biggest precision leak found wasn't the GST split — it was `pos_checkout.go`'s cart-total accumulation.** `totalSale += int(item.SalePrice) * item.Qty` truncated the **unit price** to whole rupees *before* multiplying by quantity, so a fractional-rupee item lost its fraction on every unit sold, not once per sale. Fixed alongside the GST fix (`RupeesToPaise(item.SalePrice) * int64(item.Qty)`, summed as paise). The identical bug pattern existed in `fulfillment.go`'s `ProcessReturnAnywhere` and was fixed the same way. **If a future money computation multiplies a per-unit price by a quantity, check whether it truncates before or after the multiply — before is wrong.**
  - **The loyalty points economy (`engines/loyalty.go`) was deliberately left untouched and NOT converted to paise.** `EarnLoyaltyPoints`'/`RedeemLoyaltyPoints`'s rupee-per-point formulas are a separate, intentional whole-rupee business rule (`loyalty.rupees_per_point` setting), not a precision bug — `pos_checkout.go` now keeps two parallel totals, `totalSalePaise`/`totalCostPaise` (precise, feeds the ledger) and `totalSaleRupees`/`totalCostRupees` (the old truncating computation, unchanged, feeds only the points-earning/redemption-cap logic exactly as before). **Do not "fix" the points system into paise without a real product reason — it would change how many points customers earn, a business decision nobody asked for here.**
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean. Provisioned a **from-scratch throwaway Postgres instance** (own port 5437, own data directory under the scratchpad, torn down after) via the embedded migration runner — all 123 migrations applied cleanly including this one, `gl_postings.debit`/`.credit` confirmed `BIGINT` via `\d`. Full `go test ./... -p 1` green across all four packages after fixing 5 pre-existing test files whose assertions read raw `gl_postings.debit`/`.credit` values and needed the same ×100 (`backdated_posting_test.go`, `gst_test.go` ×2 subtests, `journal_voucher_test.go`, `vendor_invoice_test.go`) — all were legitimate stale-assertion fixes, not test weakening (each now asserts the paise-scaled value the correct behavior actually produces). Backfill arithmetic double-checked in isolation (`BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` against a temp table seeded with legacy INT values, confirmed exact ×100, rolled back). **Live end-to-end proof of the audit's own worked example**, via a scratch server (port 9191) + a throwaway verification program: `CalculateGST(999, 18, false)` now posts CGST=8991 paise/SGST=8991 paise (₹89.91 each, ₹179.82 total, exactly correct) versus the pre-fix ₹178 the audit measured; trial balance still balanced.   - **🔴 TWO REAL BUGS WERE FOUND AND FIXED DURING LIVE VERIFICATION — READ BOTH BEFORE TOUCHING `Hold` OR ADDING A NEW VALIDATION ERROR CODE.**
    1. **HR/Admin could release a `Hold` by editing it directly through the generic doc endpoint, completely bypassing the `HoldReleaseRequest` maker-checker gate.** `role_permissions.allow_update=FALSE` was set on every role including HR/Admin, and that was **not enough** — HR/Admin bypasses the generic doc endpoint's RBAC check entirely (confirmed live: a Store Manager got `GLOBAL-0011` on the same request, HR/Admin's identical request succeeded). This is the same systemwide override `DecideApproval`'s own location check already documents (`!IsSuperAdmin(actorRole)`), and it is not itself a bug — it's a real gap in relying on `role_permissions` alone to gate a sensitive transition. **Fixed with `validateHoldMasterRules`**, a doctype validator (`ValidateMasterDataRules`'s switch) that runs for every caller with no admin bypass — refuses any save that moves `Hold.status` from Active to Released. `ReleaseHold`'s own direct-SQL status write (only reachable from `DecideApproval` on an Approved `HoldReleaseRequest`) never goes through this validator, so the legitimate path still works. **If a future doctype needs an approval-gated transition that isn't the document's own `status` field going through the standard Draft→Pending Approval→Approved/Rejected machinery (which GLOBAL-0019 already protects, unconditionally), it needs its own validator like this one — `role_permissions` is not sufficient by itself.**
    2. **A new business-rule validation error can silently become a bare 500 with zero log line.** `error_catalog_generated.go` is generated from `ERP_Standard_Message_Control_Matrix_Final.xlsx` (`DO NOT EDIT BY HAND`, see its own header) — a `*ValidationError` whose `Code` isn't a key in that generated map hits `writeAPIErrorDetail`'s `!ok` branch, which returns a `GLOBAL-0302`/500 **before ever calling `logForEntry`**. Confirmed live: minting a new `GOODSR-0098` code for the catch-weight check produced a 500 with *no corresponding server log entry at all* — the silent failure mode is exactly why it wasn't caught by inspection. **Every new business-rule check added anywhere in this codebase must reuse an existing catalog code** (this Stage used `GLOBAL-0002`, `META-0198`, `META-0199`, `MASTER-0053` throughout — the generic ones) **unless the xlsx itself is updated and `scripts/gen_error_catalog.py` re-run.** Grep `errorCatalog\[` / `writeAPIErrorDetail` before assuming a hand-picked code string will work.
  - **`computeATS`'s signature changed** (`engines/inventory.go`): gained a trailing `held int` parameter, subtracted like every other blocking bucket. All 6 call sites (`inventory.go` x2, `oms_reports.go`, `public_api_v1.go`, `sourcing.go` x2) updated to select the new `inventory_availability.hold_qty` column and pass it through — **if a 7th call site is ever added, it must select `hold_qty` too or it will silently treat every held unit as available.**
  - **`Hold` is deliberately not creatable/editable through the generic doc API at all** (`allow_create`/`allow_update` FALSE for every role) — the only way in is `PlaceHold` (`POST /api/v1/wms/hold/place`) and, for release, an approved `HoldReleaseRequest`. It still shows up read-only in the ordinary doctype-table browser (Holds in the sidebar).
  - **`ReceiptValidationRule`/catch-weight/hazmat all attach to *existing* choke points rather than new parallel checks** — `getReceiptValidationRule` feeds the existing PURCHA-0087/0088 checks inside `validateGRNRules`, `ValidateReceiptCatchWeightLine` sits in the same per-line loop as the 42.1.4/42.1.8 batch/serial checks, and `checkHazmatCompatibility` lives inside `PutawayToBin` itself so cross-dock/planned-cross-dock inherit it automatically. No rule/flag configured anywhere reproduces every pre-Stage tenant's exact prior behavior — verified live at the 0%/24-vs-25-unit tolerance boundary specifically, not just spot-checked.
  - **RF Receiving (42.3.10) resolves a scanned/typed value directly against `expected_items[].sku`, with no barcode→SKU lookup step** — this codebase's barcodes already encode the SKU code itself (confirmed by re-reading `addSKUToPOSCart`, which does the same thing for POS scan-to-cart). **Do not add a barcode-resolution API for this screen without first confirming that assumption no longer holds** for whatever prompted the question.
  - **The generic doc endpoint replaces the whole `data` blob on every save — there is no partial-field PATCH.** `patchYardCheckIn` (app.js) fetches the full record before patching two fields and reposting it, mirroring `editDocRecord`'s own fetch-then-resend convention. **Any new quick-action button that edits an existing document must do the same** — a naive `{status: "X"}`-only POST silently blanks every other field on that document.

  - **⚠️ `Bin.zone` deliberately did NOT become a `Link` to `Zone`, despite the plan's own literal wording — this resolves open decision 42.D6.** A Link's value must be `documents.id`, but every existing Bin holds a human zone code, so a real Link would have forced a destructive rewrite of every live Bin's `zone` value plus broken every existing zone-display consumer (pick lists, slotting suggestions), which would then show an opaque id instead of a code. `Bin.zone` stays `Data`, validated against `Zone.code` by a new `validateBinMasterRules` (Bin had none before). **Do not "fix" this into a real Link** — it would be the fourth time this Stage explicitly chose Data-plus-code-validation over Link for exactly this reason (Batch.item, SerialNumber.item, UOMConversion.item, now Zone).
  - **⚠️ `AllocationStrategy` can never override FEFO for a batch-tracked item, and that is a correctness gate, not an oversight to loosen.** `ResolveAllocationStrategy` checks `tracking.TracksBatch()` before ever consulting a configured strategy; FEFO is not even a selectable value on the master (the Select fieldtype's own options list refuses it at `META-0199`, before the master's own validation runs). Only a non-batch-tracked item's previously-hardcoded FIFO default is now configurable, among FIFO/LIFO/NearestBin/FewestPicks/CleanLocation.
  - **⚠️ Moving a `WarehouseTask` into `Exception` now requires a real Active `ReasonCode` of category `WMS Exception` — free text no longer works, and this is a real behaviour change, not purely additive.** Acceptable because `WarehouseTask` only shipped in the prior session (42.2.1) and had zero live Exception transitions to break. `applyExceptionFollowOn` actually fires the one follow-on action with a real mechanism (**Create Count Task** → `CreateWarehouseTask`, live-verified end-to-end); Reallocate/Hold have no automatic mechanism anywhere in this codebase, so both only write an audit event naming what a human must still do — **do not invent a reallocation/hold API to "complete" this** without a real product ask for it first.

Fixed at the one choke point, `renderView`: the scratch container is now attached off-screen (`position:absolute; left:-99999px`, deliberately not `display:none`, which breaks `position:sticky` table-header layout per Stage 45's own earlier note) rather than fully detached — `document.getElementById` now resolves correctly during every render, and the anti-flicker/anti-race behaviour Stage 45 built is unchanged. **If `renderView`'s container strategy is touched again, re-run the same three-screen Playwright check before trusting it** — a fully-detached container looks correct in a diff and only fails at runtime.

FRONTEND ONLY, UNCOMMITTED, NOT DEPLOYED, live verification in progress.** User reported 8 issues live against screenshots of the `:8080` deployment (Financial Accounting flyout duplicating panels, F&A frozen header, help icon styling, Wholeops rebrand ask, Setup submenu group-label/seam visuals, PIM tab bar clipping at 90% zoom, setup banner dismiss behaviour), then asked for a broader design-bar sweep and to "fix all." Modified: `public/app.js`, `public/styles.css`, `public/index.html`. **No Go changes, no migration, no new dependency.** Full per-item detail is in `docs/micro_checklist.md` Stage 45; summary here:
  - **⚠️ THE DUPLICATE-PANEL BUG WAS A ROUTER RACE, NOT A PER-SCREEN BUG — READ THIS BEFORE TOUCHING `renderView` AGAIN.** `renderView()`/`renderViewContent()` (app.js) previously appended each view's content straight into the live `#view-root`; two overlapping calls (double-click, or any duplicate trigger firing before the first call's fetch resolved) both landed their output there permanently. Fixed with a `viewRenderToken` counter: content now builds into a **detached scratch container** and is swapped into `#view-root` only if the call's token is still current when its fetch resolves — a stale call's work is discarded, never touching the visible DOM. `renderViewContent`'s signature changed from `(view)` to `(view, root)` — **do not add a second call site that invokes `renderViewContent` directly without going through `renderView`**, the token/scratch-container machinery lives entirely in the caller. The existing Chromium sticky-header-caching workaround and the `translateDOM` sweep both moved from the end of `renderViewContent` into `renderView`'s token-gated `finally`, since they need the real root post-swap.
  - **⚠️ CORRECTED A MID-SESSION MISDIAGNOSIS — WORTH KNOWING IF THE F&A FROZEN-HEADER REPORT RESURFACES.** Initial hypothesis blamed `.table-wrapper { contain: paint }` for Chart of Accounts' header floating through scrolled rows. That's wrong: `renderChartOfAccountsPanel` doesn't use `.table-wrapper` at all (bare `.table-panel > table`), so that rule can't reach it. Checked the actual local code instead of the theory: the Stage 41.2 `th`-level sticky fix and the Chromium-caching `requestAnimationFrame` workaround are both present and correct here. **No code change was made for this one.** Most likely explanation is that the `:8080` deployment the screenshots were taken against simply predates one of those two fixes — confirm by redeploying and re-checking live, don't assume a second bug exists until that's ruled out.
  - **PIM/Finance/Reports/HR/Manufacturing tab bars now scroll (`overflow-x: auto`) instead of `.page-container`'s `overflow-x: hidden` silently clipping trailing tabs** — reported as PIM's "Supplier Submissions" tab being completely unreachable at 90% zoom, no scrollbar, no recovery path except knowing the direct URL. **⚠️ The first version of this fix was live-verified and found to make it WORSE — the tab bar collapsed to `height: 0`, fully invisible.** `.page-container` is `flex-direction: column`; `overflow-x: auto` alone suppresses the item's automatic min-height (content-based sizing only applies when overflow is `visible`, and the CSS Overflow spec forces the *used* value of the other axis to `auto` regardless of what's declared), so the bar had nothing stopping it shrinking to nothing whenever the page's other content overflowed vertically. **If you touch these 5 tab bars again: the fix that actually works is `flex-shrink: 0`** — it exempts the item from shrinking at all instead of trying to win the automatic-minimum-size calculation. `overflow-y: visible` is also set alongside it but **does not itself prevent the collapse** — live-measured via `getComputedStyle`, it still reports `overflow-y: auto` even with `visible` explicitly declared (the spec's axis-pairing rule fires on computed value, explicit-`visible` or default-`visible` makes no difference). Don't trust that line to do anything; `flex-shrink: 0` is the one holding this up. Re-verified live after the correction (measured 46px bar height at narrow width vs. 0px before).
  - **Setup-prerequisite banner (e.g. HR > Attendance's "needs setup first") is no longer dismissible** — the × and its `sessionStorage` dismiss-tracking (`setupBannerDismissed`/`dismissSetupBanner`/`SETUP_BANNER_DISMISS_KEY`) are removed outright, per explicit user direction that this should show on every visit while the prerequisite is genuinely unmet, not just be a Stage-41-era session-scoped dismiss.
  - **Setup flyout submenu**: group headers (CRM/CORE/FINANCE/…) got a solid background chip (`.submenu-group-label`); the sticky "Filter setup lists…" search box got the same `box-shadow: inset` seal Stage 41.2 already uses on `th`, since it had the identical border-only-leaks-at-fractional-zoom bug.
  - **New `.btn-icon` modifier** for icon-only buttons (currently just `#help-btn`) — `.btn`'s default padding is sized for an icon+label pair and stretched a lone icon into an off-centre rectangle.
  - **New standalone sidebar item "Knowledge Center"** (after Reports) → `renderView('help')`. Previously the only paths in were the per-screen "?" drawer's footer link or knowing the `/help` URL directly — not in the sidebar, not in `buildGlobalSearchIndex()` (which reads the sidebar at build time, so this one addition also makes it searchable, no second registration needed).

  - **⚠️ THIS FIXED A LIVE CORRECTNESS BUG THAT 37.1.2 SHIPPED, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS ENTRY.** `PostSalesInvoice` read `total_amount` — on a USD invoice that is 1000, in *dollars* — and posted it straight into Accounts Receivable. **A USD 1,000 invoice at 83.00 booked a 1,000-rupee receivable instead of an 83,000 one**: receivable, ageing, credit exposure and trial balance each understated by 98.8%, silently. `PayVendorInvoice` had the same defect. Both now read the `base_*` twin 37.1.2 already stamps. **If any tenant posted a foreign-currency invoice between 37.1.2 and this fix, its GL is wrong and needs a correcting journal** — nothing here backfills that, and it should not, because a silent retroactive GL rewrite is worse than a named correction.
  - **⚠️ ONE NEW MIGRATION, AND THE `//go:embed *.sql` TRAP APPLIES.** Committing `migrations_stage37_1_fx_revaluation.sql` *is* scheduling it. It seeds four GL accounts (4200 Realised FX Gain, 4210 Unrealised FX Gain, 5600 Realised FX Loss, 5610 Unrealised FX Loss) into `tenant_default` and backfills existing tenant schemas. It adds **no table and no column** — revaluation state lives as engine-written JSON keys on the document. Applied to dev (`1 applied; 95 already up to date`); prod has not seen it.
  - **🔒 The whole feature is INERT for a single-currency tenant, and that is load-bearing.** Every FX path is behind `position.Foreign`, which is false whenever a document's currency equals the tenant's functional currency — i.e. always, unless someone deliberately transacts in another currency. A functional settlement posts exactly the two lines it always did, adds no FX line, attempts no rate lookup, and even keeps its old audit-log wording. `TestFunctionalCurrencySettlementIsUnchanged` exists solely to keep that true.
  - **⚠️ THE DECISION MOST LIKELY TO BE RE-LITIGATED: realised FX is measured against the REVALUED carrying amount, not the original booked amount.** Total P&L across every period is then exactly (cash received − amount booked), whatever the split. Measuring against the original would double-count every rupee already recognised at a period end. There is deliberately **no reversing entry** on day one of the next period — this codebase has no automatic reversal machinery, and the cumulative carrying amount reaches the identical answer with one fewer moving part. `TestRealisedFXMeasuresAgainstCarryingNotBookedAmount` pins it. **Do not "simplify" it back to booked-amount arithmetic.**
  - **⚠️ The sign convention differs between AR and AP and that is not a bug.** When the foreign currency strengthens, a receivable is worth MORE (gain) and a payable costs MORE to settle (loss). `resolveSettlementFX` is *told* which side it is on rather than inferring it. Four accounts, not two, because realised and unrealised must never be summed — one is cash, the other is an opinion that can reverse.
  - **⚠️ `FXSettlement.PostingDate()` returns EMPTY unless the caller named a date, and that is deliberate.** Empty makes the closed-period check use Postgres's `CURRENT_DATE`, exactly as every settlement did before. `SettlementDate` defaults to the Go process's **UTC** date, and on this Asia/Calcutta deployment that is yesterday until 05:30 local — a settlement run at 02:00 on the 1st would otherwise be refused by the previous month's just-closed period. Same lesson as the Stage 14 lockout bug and Stage 35's SLA bug. **Do not "tidy" it into always passing the date.**
  - **The revaluation is GET-previews / POST-commits, split on the verb rather than a `dry_run` flag.** A GET cannot change the ledger however it is called, which is what makes it safe behind a button a controller presses repeatedly, and safe against a retry or double-click booking a second adjustment. Idempotent **by refusal with a named reason**, not by silent no-op: "nothing to do" and "already revalued on the 31st" send a controller to completely different places at close.
  - **What gets revalued is a closed list** (`revaluableOpenItems`): `SalesInvoice` while Approved, `VendorInvoice` while Matched/Pending Approval. **Do not widen it to every currency-bearing doctype** — PurchaseOrder, SalesOrder, RFQ and PurchaseRequisition are *commitments*, and revaluing one posts a gain against a control account holding no matching balance.
  - **⚠️ Two pre-existing bugs in 37.1.1's validation were fixed here, both found over live HTTP.** (1) An `ExchangeRate` with **no end date was unsaveable** — `fmt.Sprintf("%v", nil)` is the string `"<nil>"`, not `""`, so an omitted optional field failed a format check and the message blamed a field the user never filled in. New `currencyField` helper (delegates to the existing `payloadString`). (2) **A `Currency` whose document id is not its ISO code breaks every invoice that selects it** — `currency` is a `Link` resolving against `documents.id`, while `ApplyDocumentCurrency` demands a 3-letter ISO code, so only id == code satisfies both; the seeded INR row happens to be keyed that way, which is why nothing noticed. Now refused at authoring time. **A Currency master must be created with its ISO code as the id.**
  - **The general lesson, again**: every one of the four defects above was found by **live HTTP verification, not by the tests** — they live on the document/Link path and in columns the tests never read back. That is the same lesson Stage 42.1's entry records one day earlier.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; migration applied and `-migrate-status` clean; 22 new subtests plus `go test ./... -p 1` **fully green**. Live over HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8151, stopped and released, **all fixtures deleted and residue confirmed zero**): post at 83 (83,000 not 1,000), revalue at Closing 84 (+1,000 to 4210, previewed then committed, re-run skipped), settle at Spot 85 (cash 85,000, AR cleared at the 84,000 carrying amount, realised gain 1,000 **not** 2,000, AR netting to zero), the AP mirror producing a 2,000 loss on the same numbers, both authoring-time refusals, and all three reports. **NOT done**: no frontend surface for the two new routes (revaluation is API-only), no real-browser pass, and nothing exercised against production data.

  - **⚠️ `Batch.item` is `Data` holding the SKU CODE, deliberately not a `Link` to Item, and `batch_no` is a field rather than the document id.** The generic Link check (META-0198) resolves against `documents.id`, and an Item's id is not its code in this tree (`ITEM-<sku>`) — a Link would reject the exact value a clerk reads off a carton. And ids are globally unique across doctypes while a lot number is only unique *within* an item, so keying on `batch_no` would make the second supplier shipping "LOT-001" unsaveable. Both are checked in `validateBatchMasterRules` instead. **Do not "tidy" either into a Link or an id.**
  - **⚠️ THE BUG THE TESTS COULD NOT HAVE FOUND, and the general lesson.** The receipt gate was originally only inside `PostGRNReceiptWithQC`, which runs *after* the GRN document is written — and the GRN create hook cancels the receipt when posting fails. So a missing lot number reached the user as a GLOBAL-0302 "unexpected error" with a silently cancelled GRN behind it. Every unit test passed, because they call the engine directly and never travel the document path. **Live HTTP verification is what surfaced it.** Now `ValidateReceiptBatchLine` is called from `validateGRNRules` (pre-write) *and* kept at the posting choke point; one definition so they cannot drift. `wms_receiving.go`'s own header already stated the rule that was broken: that file owns posting effect, the validation file owns field-shape checking.
  - **`PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucher` now carries `batch_no` on the line it already posts, and its idempotency key gains the lot when there is one.** A second batch-stamped ledger entry would double-count the same physical movement in every report that sums ledger qty; and without the lot in the key, two lots of one SKU on one receipt would have had the second swallowed as a replay of the first. Keys minted before this are byte-identical (the suffix is appended only when a lot exists).
  - **Four dead message-matrix codes finally have callers**: INV-0256 (FEFO violation), INVENT-0106 (batch expired), INVENT-0104 (blocked stock), INVENT-0115 (serial/batch mismatch). They have been in `error_catalog_generated.go` since Stage 23 with nothing raising them — the matrix anticipated this feature. If you add traceability rejections, reuse these before inventing codes.
  - **The expiry sweep is an operator-triggered POST, not a background timer, on purpose.** It moves stock out of `available`, so a warehouse must be able to name a person and a time — and scheduling it is decision **42.D5** (reuse `scheduled_reports.go` vs a second timer), not something to assume. It is idempotent, so a tenant that wants it scheduled can call it from the existing job path without a second implementation.

Stage 36.2 touched only the files listed above. It is additive throughout (four new doctypes, their fields, role permissions, six indexes, and the standard existing-tenant backfill loop) and alters no existing doctype, column or row. Applied to the dev DB (`1 applied; 93 already up to date`, `-migrate-status` clean afterwards); prod has not seen it.
  - **The design decision most likely to be re-litigated: a task is not an approval, and the two must stay apart.** An approval asks "may this saved document proceed?" and is answered once by someone with the authority; a task is owned by a named person, has a due date, survives being half-done, and exists *because* the product is not ready. A task can never move a document's approval state, and no approval path was touched. If someone later proposes folding tasks into the approval engine, this is why not.
  - **`Done` and `Cancelled` are terminal on purpose, and it is a safety property.** A Done task may already have satisfied its workflow stage's exit and advanced the run past it; there is no honest way to un-advance a run whose later stages have created work. The refusal names the alternative (a follow-up task, one button in the inbox). **Do not "helpfully" add a re-open path** without first solving run rewind.
  - **The workflow condition vocabulary is closed and must stay closed.** Seven named checks, one operand each, all in `pimWorkflowConditions` in `engines/pim_workflow.go`. `ListPIMWorkflowConditions` is generated from that same map so the form cannot offer something the engine will not evaluate, and a test asserts they cannot drift. **Adding a condition is a five-line change to that map — never a user-authored expression language**, which on a form authored by a category manager is a remote-execution surface.
  - **`AdvancePIMWorkflowRun` is idempotent and is called from three places** (run creation, every task completion, an operator pressing Advance). On a run that cannot move it changes nothing but the recorded reason. Entry conditions are evaluated *before* leaving the current group, so a blocked run stays put with an explanation rather than being stranded between stages.
  - **⚠️ Known limitation:** editing a workflow definition while runs are in flight can orphan a run whose stored `current_group` no longer exists. Such a run is **stopped with a named reason**, not silently restarted (restarting would re-create every task the product has already been through). Versioning definitions for in-flight runs is the proper fix and is out of 36.2's scope.
  - **New endpoint worth knowing about:** `GET /api/v1/pim/assignable-users` returns active users' username and role only, gated on *update* rights over `PIMTask`. It exists because `/api/v1/admin/users` is Super Admin gated, so a Store Manager could not otherwise see who to assign work to. **It is not a general user-listing endpoint — do not widen it.**

Rather than touch it (renaming it even briefly risks clobbering their next Write), verification ran against an isolated copy: `git archive HEAD | tar -x` into the scratchpad, then overlay **only this session's own files**. That gave a clean `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` and — importantly — let the migration be applied without `db/migrate.go`'s `//go:embed *.sql` sweeping in their unfinished `.sql`. `-migrate-status` from that copy showed exactly one pending migration, which is the check that proves the isolation held.

Applied to the dev DB only; prod has not seen it.
  - **⚠️ THE SHARED GST BREAKDOWN DOES NOT RECONCILE TO THE PAISA, AND THAT IS NOW KNOWN RATHER THAN FIXED GLOBALLY.** `ComputeGSTForLinesMode` rounds each line's CGST/SGST halves independently then sums, so a two-line 18% order totalling 900 yields **CGST+SGST = 137.30 against its own TotalTax of 137.28**, and `TaxableAmount` is accumulated unrounded. The new invoice path fixes this **for itself** via `reconcileInvoiceAmounts`. It was deliberately **not** applied to the shared engine, whose contract is that every existing sale-side caller (POS checkout included) stays byte-identical. **If you touch GST rounding, do not "unify" these without re-verifying POS totals** — and be aware any other document built off that breakdown has the same drift.
  - **⚠️ `CreateSalesInvoiceFromOrder`'s existence check was widened, and it is load-bearing.** It used to look only for its own `INV-<orderID>`; the handover cascade calls it on the Shipped transition, so without the widening **every package-invoiced order would be invoiced a second time, in full**. Do not narrow it back.
  - **The invoice→label→manifest ordering rule binds only bookings that HAVE a shipping package.** A pre-35.4 or manual booking labels and manifests exactly as before. That exemption is deliberate and is covered by its own test — do not "tighten" it into an unconditional rule without a backfill, or every legacy booking becomes unlabellable.
  - **Inter-state cannot be reliably derived, and the default is intra-state.** `SalesOrder` has no state field, only free-text `shipping_address`; parsing it would be wrong often enough to be worse than useless *on a tax document*. Resolution is explicit override → the order's `shipping_state`/`custom_shipping_state` → default intra-state, with the basis recorded on the invoice as `gst_basis`. A real `shipping_state` on SalesOrder is the fix and belongs with 35.6's channel adapters.
  - **Test-suite ceiling worth knowing**: `db.InitDB` reassigns the global pool without closing the previous one, so every call leaks idle connections. The package convention is one `InitDB` per test, and it was already close enough to Postgres's `max_connections` that adding one 12-test file tipped `go test ./... -p 1` into `sorry, too many clients already`. That is a **connection ceiling, not a logic failure** — if you see it, count `InitDB` calls before hunting a bug. `shipping_package_test.go` reuses a live pool (`spInitDB`); the other files were left alone.

  - **✅ THE DEV CLUSTER'S WIN1252 PROBLEM IS FIXED (2026-08-13) — `custom_erp` and `custom_erp_test` are now UTF-8.** Background: `migrations_stage37_1_currency_foundation.sql` seeds `₹`, WIN1252 cannot represent it (SQLSTATE 22P05), and `ApplyPendingMigrations` stops at the first failure — so everything from Stage 37.1 onward was blocked. This was the 20.6 defect, not a bad migration. **Converted by dump-and-restore, not by editing the migration**: `pg_dump --encoding=UTF8` → `CREATE DATABASE ... ENCODING 'UTF8' LOCALE_PROVIDER icu ICU_LOCALE 'en-US' TEMPLATE template0` → restore → `ALTER DATABASE ... RENAME`. **ICU `en-US` deliberately, not `C`** — `C` collation would silently switch every text `ORDER BY` in the app to byte order (uppercase before lowercase). Proven lossless before the swap: 127 tables with identical exact row counts plus a matching `md5` checksum over `tenant_default.documents`. All 5 blocked migrations then applied and the full suite is green against it.
    - **The WIN1252 originals are RETAINED as `custom_erp_win1252_backup` and `custom_erp_test_win1252_backup`.** Nothing was dropped, so this is reversible with two renames. Drop them when you are satisfied: `DROP DATABASE custom_erp_win1252_backup; DROP DATABASE custom_erp_test_win1252_backup;`
    - **✅ `template1` was recreated as UTF-8 on 2026-08-13, so a bare `CREATE DATABASE` on this box is now born UTF8 + ICU `en-US`.** Verified by creating one with no encoding clause at all and storing `₹`/CJK/emoji in it. `template1` was pristine (no user objects beyond `plpgsql`), so the drop-and-recreate from `template0` touched no data. **`template0` is still WIN1252 and that is correct** — it cannot be re-encoded without `initdb` and does not need to be, since it is precisely the template a *differing* encoding may legally be copied from, which is what makes every explicit `TEMPLATE template0 ENCODING 'UTF8'` clause in this repo work. The `postgres` maintenance DB is also still WIN1252, deliberately left alone: it holds only 20 empty tables and one `test-tenant` row left over from the retired standalone WMS project, and it is not what new databases are copied from. `db/migrate.go` still recognises 22P05 and explains it if this ever bites again.
    - **This uncovered how bad the reservation leak actually was**: `custom_erp` held **5,395 orphaned reservations out of 5,419** — 99.6% of every reservation in it was holding stock against a line that no longer wanted it. The 35.3.7 sweeper drained it to 0 with no negative `reserved` counts.

All three are additive (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, new tables, partial indexes) and idempotent, and none changes existing behaviour: the public-API tables are inert until a key is issued, the currency columns are NULL for every existing posting, and the reservation columns are NULL for every existing row (the release path falls back to the old heuristic for exactly those). Check `erp-server -migrate-status` before and after.
  - **🔒 The public API is live but reaches only four read-only endpoints.** `/api/public/v1/{items,items/{code},inventory,orders/{id}/status}`. `publicAPIMiddleware` **panics at registration** if a route declares no scope, so a route cannot become public by accident. It is a *second* middleware, deliberately not a branch in `apiMiddleware`: a human session presents a JWT and inherits a role, an integration presents an opaque key and holds only its explicit scopes. **Do not "simplify" by merging them** — that is exactly how a public route inherits a human's permissions.
  - **⚠️ `gl_postings.debit`/`credit` still mean FUNCTIONAL currency and must keep meaning that.** Every report, trial balance, P&L, GST return and reconciliation sums those two columns and none of them knows about currency. 37.1.2's transaction amounts live in the new `transaction_debit`/`transaction_credit`/`currency`/`exchange_rate` columns beside them, NULL for a functional-currency posting. **If you add an FX feature, do not start writing foreign amounts into `debit`/`credit`.**
  - **⚠️ The reservation sweeper must never expire an order's reservation on time alone.** `inventory.reservation_ttl_seconds` is a *cart-hold* TTL. Applying it to a confirmed order releases stock out from under live orders the moment they queue longer than the TTL — worse than the permanent-reservation leak it fixes. Unattributed holds expire on `expires_at`; attributed ones only when their line is gone, deleted or no longer holding stock. The test asserts that negative case first, on purpose.

  - **The Assist button never posts, by design.** 26.4.11's whole safety argument is that a human reviews the draft and saves it as an ordinary Draft through the existing approval gate. The button fills form fields and stops. It also refuses to overwrite non-empty fields without a confirm.

  - **⚠️ A real security defect was found and fixed — 43.1, token claim delimiter smuggling.** The `k=v&k=v` claims payload was built by `fmt.Sprintf` from columns with no character restrictions, so a `&`/`=` in a username or `location_code` reshaped the payload *and the server then signed it*. Four proven consequences: a smuggled `purpose=extension` made `apiMiddleware` skip the Stage 29.8 live user-state re-check (**deactivating that account stopped ending its session**); a `location_code` overrode `role` and `tenant` (both emitted before `loc`, last-write-wins); an MFA-challenge token could be given the `role` claim `SignPurposeToken` deliberately omits; and an `=` in a value dropped the claim entirely, emptying `Resolved-Username` in the audit trail. Both write paths are `requireHRAdmin`-gated, so severity is insider persistence/escalation, not anonymous remote. Fixed with `url.QueryEscape`/`QueryUnescape` at the single choke point all three issuers share, plus `SplitN`. **Backward-compatible** — unescape failures fall back to the literal so tokens minted before this keep verifying.
  - **This was one of `pentest_scope.md` §4's own named P1s.** That doc now marks it REMEDIATED so a vendor does not bill for rediscovering it. The second is not redundant: **26.11.3 found production had never run a nightly backup at all**, and a job that never fires cannot report its own failure. Deliberately NOT on `/api/v1/health` — that route is public and would hand out backup paths and schedule.

    - **SLA breach detection had never fired.** `documents.created_at` is a bare `TIMESTAMP` holding the DB server's *local* wall clock; `lib/pq` hands it back **tagged UTC**. `GetSLABreaches`' `time.Since(created_at)` therefore saw every open task as 330 minutes in the *future* on this Asia/Calcutta box, so nothing younger than 5.5h + the threshold ever breached. Now computed in SQL (`NOW() - created_at`). Live proof: the console's SLA tile reads **230**; it would have read 0. **This trap applies to every `time.Since(someTimestampColumn)` in this codebase** — if you find another, it is the same bug. The `TestEngines/AdvancedOptimizationAndForecasting` fixture was inserting `time.Now().UTC()`, a frame nothing else writes; it only ever agreed with the broken reader, and now inserts local time like `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` does.

The real config lives in `deploy/Caddyfile` and is installed by `sudo bash /opt/erp/deploy/enable_tls.sh <domain> <email>`, which checks DNS resolves to the box *before* touching anything (a premature ACME run burns Let's Encrypt's weekly failure quota), validates the generated config, and auto-rolls-back a failed reload. A plain `source` leaves vars unexported and every child script (`migrate.sh`, `backup.sh`) dies on its `${DATABASE_URL:?}` guard. Use `set -a; . /etc/erp/erp.env; set +a`. **This trap has now bitten twice** — `deploy.ps1` on 2026-08-04, and the nightly backup cron on 2026-08-07, where the broken `source ... && backup.sh` form had been sitting in `backup.sh`'s header comment and in `deploy/README.md`'s hand-paste instructions since day one. All known copies are fixed; the point to carry forward is that **you cannot fix this by adding `export` to `erp.env`** — systemd's `EnvironmentFile=` rejects an `export ` prefix, so that trades a broken cron for a service that will not boot. A temporary `* * * * *` entry against a scratch `BACKUP_DIR` fired on its own (`CRON[131799]: (erp) CMD ...`) and produced a valid backup; entry and scratch removed. Wiring this to 20.2's alert webhook is now the highest-value outstanding backup action. Off-box copies remain deliberately absent (no storage credentials): **losing the droplet still loses the backups.**

Detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 41 and `project_ledger.md` §89. Things the next session needs to know:
  - **⚠️ RENUMBERED FROM 34.4 → 41, and this invalidates several references written the same day.** This work was labelled Stage 34.4 while in flight, and the concurrent Stage 40 session referenced it by that number in §88, in `micro_checklist.md` 40.2.1, in `engines/oms_console.go`, and in four bullets of this section. **34.4 is the *gated* JSON-endpoint harvester in Stage 34, blocked behind 34.6's legal/ToS decision** — leaving this work under that number would read as the gate having been crossed. All code comments and cross-references found have been corrected to 41; if you find a stray "Stage 34.4" attached to phone/setup-advisor code, it is this, not the harvester.
  - **⚠️ The migration was renamed too**: `migrations_stage34_4_country_phone_setup.sql` → `migrations_stage41_country_phone_setup.sql`. The dev DB carries ledger rows for **both** names (the old one was applied before the rename — see the `//go:embed *.sql` trap notes below, which caught this file twice). It is fully idempotent (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` / `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`), so the re-run under the new name applied cleanly. **Not on prod under either name.** It adds `Customer.phone_country`, `SalesOrder.customer_phone`/`customer_country`, and `Location.short_code` — all optional, no backfill, no destructive change.
  - **The cleaning/validation split is the design, do not collapse it.** `NormalizePhone` **never** rejects: it returns a cleaned number plus `Valid`/`Reason` and lets the caller decide. Masters are strict (Customer keeps MASTER-0051, now country-aware); `CreateSalesOrder` cleans and tags but **never rejects or holds** over a phone number. That asymmetry is deliberate — the three hold codes answer "can this be fulfilled?", and a contact number cannot, so a fourth gate there would only block real revenue. **Do not add one.**
  - **`<field>_country` is written by the server and must never be re-derived downstream.** The country is captured in the pass that can still see the `+1`; once the dial code is stripped, a US and an Indian 10-digit number are indistinguishable. A validation pass that re-parses the cleaned digits silently retags every foreign customer as domestic — that bug was live and is fixed, with `TestNormalizeDocumentPhonesKeepsForeignCountry` pinning it. It is written **only where the doctype declares the companion field**, so declaring it is the whole opt-in.
  - **`IsDerivedCompanionField` is shared with Stage 40.2's field formats on purpose.** `phone_country` holds `"US"` and substring matching saw "phone" in the name, so the server rejected a value it had just written itself. Both `DetectFieldFormat` and `IsPhoneField` now consult it, and the suffix list is served to the frontend with the format tokens. **If you add a derived companion with a new suffix, add it there**, or it will be format-checked as its parent's type.
  - **`CreateSalesOrder`'s signature changed** from seven positional strings to `engines.SalesOrderInput`. Six call sites updated (two production, four in `engines_test.go`). It was already four consecutive strings; an eighth was a transposition bug waiting to happen.
  - **The sticky-header fix contradicts a comment that said it was unfixable — read the new comment before "reverting" it.** `position: sticky` moved from `thead` (a row group, which stays in the table paint layer whatever its position) to the `<th>` cells (which join the positioned-descendants layer). The four things the old comment listed as tried all fought the paint order instead of leaving it.
  - **Two single doors carry all the setup guidance**: `attachLinkTypeahead` (~45 pickers) and `renderView` (every screen). A new screen gets hints and the banner for free. `VIEW_SETUP_PREREQS` in `app.js` is the only per-screen list, and it is deliberately short — a banner naming eight things is the nag this was built to avoid.
  - **`#/setup/<Doctype>` and `#/view/<view>` are now real routes.** Before this the app had no addressable views at all. `saveNavState` keeps the hash in step via `replaceState` (not a hash assignment — that would re-enter `hashchange` and double-render, and would push a history entry per click).
  - **Fixed a latent panic while here**: `rawSettingForSchema` dereferenced a nil `db.DB` (`database/sql` panics rather than erroring), despite its own contract saying a config read must never crash a request path. Found because a pure-function unit test consulted a setting.
  - **Left deliberately not done**: the `Location.short_code` field is created and indexed and the picker searches it, but **no existing Location record has one** — it is optional and nothing keys off it. And `attachCodeNamePicker` is applied to the two POS location fields only; the other ~13 screens that pick a location still show the code. That was scoped as the user asked (POS), and the helper is generic so the rest can be converted one at a time.
  - **Dev-DB state: clean.** Every fixture created during verification was removed — six `CUST-S344-*` Customers, three `Stage344Test`-channel `SalesOrder`s, and one throwaway `Brand` schema field. The scratch server on `:8137` was stopped and the port released. The only lasting change is the migration.

Both are additive/guarded and idempotent. **The role rename is safe to run against a live box with sessions open** *because* `engines.IsSuperAdmin` accepts both names permanently — nothing is gated on the migration having happened. Do not "clean up" the legacy alias in `engines/roles.go`; that is what makes an in-flight token, an external script, or a restored older snapshot keep working.
  - **The role rename touched 37 call sites and they all went through one predicate.** `role != "HR/Admin"` across nine files is now `!engines.IsSuperAdmin(role)`. If a future rename is needed it is one edit. Note specifically that `RequiresMFA` was moved **off** a `map[string]bool` keyed on the literal — a map lookup would have silently returned false for a legacy-named session and dropped MFA from exactly the accounts that most need it.
  - **`total_amount` on a PurchaseOrder is still the taxable value, deliberately.** `engines.PostDoubleEntry` and the existing procurement reports read it as such. The new tax-inclusive payable is a **separate** `grand_total` field. Do not "fix" `total_amount` to be the gross.
  - **`ComputeGSTForLines` was NOT changed** — a new `ComputeGSTForLinesMode` carries the inclusive/exclusive switch and the old name delegates with `inclusive=true`. Every sale-side caller (POS checkout, sales invoice, returns) is byte-identical on purpose; only the PO path opts into Exclusive.
  - **Field-format validation is at `ValidateDocument`, which `CreateSalesOrder` does not call.** That is why Stage 41's "an order is never rejected for a bad phone number" promise is untouched — orders write through their own INSERT path. The format layer only gates the generic-doc/master-data path, which is where the user wanted the guidance.
  - **The CSP got tighter, not just different.** Removing `styles.css`'s Google Fonts `@import` let `style-src`/`font-src` drop their two `https://fonts.g*.com` exceptions. If a future change re-adds a web font, the CSP has to be re-widened or the font silently will not load.
  - **`index.html`'s cache-busters are now `styles.css?v=19` / `app.js?v=24`.** All three frontend files changed. Bump again on the next frontend change or the fix ships invisibly — and note the new `Cache-Control: immutable` on versioned assets makes that *more* important than before, not less.

  - **`handleMFAActivate` and `handleMFAVerify` now return richer JSON** (`recovery_codes` array; `used_recovery_code`/`recovery_codes_remaining`). Two existing tests decoded activate into `map[string]string` and broke — both moved to `map[string]interface{}`. **Any other client decoding those responses into a string map will break the same way.**
  - **MFA re-auth on the self-service recovery screens is by PASSWORD, not TOTP, and that is deliberate.** The user who needs "regenerate my codes" or "move to a new phone" is the one whose authenticator is already gone; gating those on a TOTP code would make them useless in exactly the situation they exist for. Do not "harden" it by requiring a code.
  - **`users.mfa_pending_secret` must stay a separate column from `mfa_secret`.** It is what lets the existing authenticator keep working until the new device is confirmed. Reusing `mfa_secret` for the in-progress secret would mean an abandoned device switch *causes* the lockout this whole item exists to prevent — asserted by `TestMFAReenrollMovesAuthenticator`.
  - **Supplier row-scoping lives in `handleGenericDoc` and must stay there.** Three enforcement points (list query, single-doc read, write path) keyed off `role == "Supplier"`. Doctype-level RBAC alone does **not** confine a supplier to their own rows — every supplier holds identical permissions on `SupplierSubmission`. On create the supplier code is *overwritten* from the session; on update the *stored* code is checked, never the payload's. If you add another externally-facing role, extend this, do not copy it.
  - **An approved `SupplierSubmission` becomes `ProductContent` as Draft, never Approved.** Making it Approved would let an outside party's text reach a live sales channel with no internal publish decision. `media_url` is deliberately **not** fetched server-side on approval — that would be an SSRF surface.
  - **The login rate limiter is 5 requests/minute per IP, and `httptest.NewRequest` gives every request the same `RemoteAddr`.** Several MFA tests in a row therefore fail on **429** rather than on anything real. `doRequestFromIP` + `mfaTestIP()` in `mfa_recovery_test.go` exist solely for that; reuse them for any new test that logs in more than once.
  - **`renderView` is now a wrapper around `renderViewContent`.** The wrapper owns clearing the root and the loading placeholder; the dispatch chain moved into the inner function untouched. If you add a view, add it to `renderViewContent`. The placeholder is removed in a `finally` so a throwing renderer cannot strand a permanent "Loading…".

Adding a further printable document is one more case in that switch — do not add a route.
