  - **Only a `Paid` cart prints a receipt** (`GLOBAL-0019`, 422). An invoice prints at any status but is stamped `DRAFT` unless Approved/Paid. If you relax either, say why — the first exists so nobody walks out with a receipt for uncollected money.
  - **`isESCPOS` is intentionally narrower than `isRawLanguage`.** ZPL/TSPL have no continuous feed, so a receipt sent to a ZPL unit as raw commands prints blank. Everything but ESC-POS is rasterised as HTML. Don't "simplify" the two predicates together.
  - **An unset `label_width_mm` means 42 columns (80mm roll).** A 58mm till needs `label_width_mm = 58` or lines lay out too wide. `escposRow` clips the *description* and never the amount.
  - **Two pre-existing bugs were fixed here, both on the receipt path.** `printPOSReceipt` never deducted `offer_discount`, so a sale with a Stage 30.7 offer printed a total **higher than the cash collected**; and `#receipt-print-area` had no on-screen `display: none` (only `@media print` ever hid it), so a printed receipt stayed visible at the bottom of the live page. If either reappears, check the 7th `printPOSReceipt` argument and the `#sticker-print-area, #receipt-print-area, #invoice-print-area` rule in `styles.css`.
  - **`qzTryPrint` has a `quiet` option and the three new call sites all pass it.** Without it a cashier gets a `DEVICE-0298` modal on *every sale* in any tenant that has not configured a Receipt printer, which is the normal un-set-up state. Quiet suppresses only the payload-preparation and unmapped-printer dialogs; a tray-side failure stays loud.

- **UNCOMMITTED (2026-08-04) — Stage 33, dialog viewport fit + responsive form layout.** Frontend only: `public/styles.css` (the `.modal-*` and `.custom-dialog-*` blocks) and `public/app.js` (`openDynamicModal`, two small additions). No Go, no schema, no migration, no new files. Shares `public/app.js` and `public/styles.css` with the uncommitted Stage 31.1 and 32.1 work below — **check `git status` and stage the specific files you reviewed.** Full detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 33 and `project_ledger.md` §79. Things worth knowing before touching it:
  - **The user's request was "make it 2-3 columns"; the actual bug was that no dialog in the app could scroll.** `.modal-container` had no `max-height` and no scroll region, and `body` is `overflow: hidden` — so a form taller than the viewport was centred by the overlay's flexbox and clipped off **both** ends, Save button included. If a dialog ever looks truncated again, check the flex chain before touching the grid.
  - **`min-height: 0` on the scrolling body and `.modal-container > form` are both load-bearing.** Most dialogs wrap body+footer in a `<form>`, so the form — not `.modal-body` — is the container's flex child; without the `> form` rule the body never gets leftover height and the footer stays clipped. Without `min-height: 0` a flex child will not shrink below its content and the container overflows again. Removing either silently restores the original bug.
  - **The responsive behaviour is intrinsic, not breakpointed — do not add media queries for zoom levels.** `#dynamic-modal-body` is `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(240px, 100%), 1fr))` and the height bound is `calc(100dvh - 40px)`. Browser zoom only changes the CSS-pixel viewport, so 40%/100%/120%/200% are all handled by the same two rules; a breakpoint list would just be a thing to forget.
  - **The grid is scoped to `#dynamic-modal-body` on purpose.** Table/summary modals share `.modal-body` and must stay block-flow — gridding `.modal-body` globally would put a one-child table into the first of three tracks. Same reason `.modal-container-wide` (920px) is set by `openDynamicModal` only above 4 fields: a 3-field master in a 920px box is two-thirds empty row, and at 520px the 240px minimum yields one column by itself.
  - **`index.html`'s `?v=` cache-busters must be bumped whenever `app.js` or `styles.css` changes.** They are now `styles.css?v=15` / `app.js?v=20`. Stage 32.1 changed `app.js` and did *not* bump it, so a browser holding `?v=19` would have served the pre-fix file — the fix ships invisibly and looks like it did not work. Check this before concluding a frontend change "didn't take".
  - **Composite editors need `.form-group-wide`.** The JSONTable/JSONMap line editors (30.5.3) get it in `openDynamicModal`; any future field that renders a table rather than one input needs it too, or it gets squeezed into a 240px track.

A second harness served the real `index.html`+`app.js` over HTTP and confirmed `add-field`/`edit-prefix`/`add-label`/`bulk-import` still measure `cols=1 display=flex w=520`. **One trap if you rebuild that harness**: with no backend the login screen sits at `z-index: 500` over the dialogs, so *screenshots* of the real page show the login card — assert geometry via `getBoundingClientRect`, which is unaffected. - **UNCOMMITTED (2026-08-04) — Stage 32.1, sidebar hover responsiveness.** Frontend only: `public/app.js` (the flyout block, ~lines 2213-2500) and `public/styles.css`. No Go, no schema, no migration, no new files. Sits in the same tree as the uncommitted Stage 31.1 work below and shares `public/app.js` with it — **stage them separately, and check `git status` first**; the two changes do not overlap in the file but a bare `git commit` would sweep both. Full detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 32 and `project_ledger.md` §78. Things worth knowing before touching it:
  - **Do not "restore" a longer dwell to fix a menu that feels twitchy.** The delays got *shorter* (switch 140→60ms, aim grace 500→220ms, hide 260→200ms) because the test that chose between them was fixed. `pointerAimingAtOpenFlyout()` used to ask `dx > 0 && r.left >= x`; the panel always sits right of the sidebar, so the second half was true for essentially every pointer position in the nav and the whole thing collapsed to one frame of jitter. Half of all module hovers therefore waited 500ms **at random**, and if you moved toward where the menu should be during that wait you left the row, which cancelled the pending open and scheduled the hide — so no menu appeared at all and clicking the arrow was the only thing that worked. That was the user's report, verbatim.
  - **The aim test now needs real geometry to work**: velocity over a 90ms sample window, ≥12px of horizontal travel, and the projected crossing point inside the panel's vertical span (±24px). If you change the flyout's position or size, this test reads `getBoundingClientRect()` live, so it follows — but if you ever make the panel open to the *left*, the `runway = r.left - x` sign convention has to be revisited.
  - **A straight synthetic pointer path cannot reproduce the old bug.** `page.mouse.move()` in a line has `dx == 0`, so the first version of the verification harness passed against the broken code. Reproducing it needs a jittering pointer. If you write a UI test for hover behaviour here, add wobble or you are testing nothing.
  - **`--transition-speed` (0.25s) is the *layout* duration; `--transition-fast`/`--transition-med` are for interaction feedback.** Hover states used to run at 250ms, which reads as the UI lagging the cursor. Don't point new hover/press rules at `--transition-speed`, and prefer named properties over `transition: all` (`all` was animating padding and border-width on `.menu-item.active`).
  - ~~**~20 `transition: all var(--transition-speed)` sites remain**~~ — **superseded 2026-08-05 (32.2, now closed).** The estimate was stale: 11 remained, and all 11 were swept to explicit property lists at `--transition-fast` after being read against the rule each pairs with. The only surviving `--transition-speed` use is `.sidebar { transition: width }`, which is correctly a layout transition.
  - **Verified live in Chromium against the real files** — 12 assertions, 5 consecutive clean runs, and the same suite re-run against `HEAD` to prove the diagnosis (jittery approach `[2,81,3,445,70,477]`ms before → `[4,1,4,2,7,4]`ms after; "hover then reach for the menu" produced no menu before, the menu after). The harness serves `public/` statically and seeds an `erp_token` — `setupModuleFlyouts()` runs synchronously at the top of `init()` before the first `await`, so the nav binds fully with no backend. **It was one-shot tooling in the scratchpad and was not kept**; rebuild it from the recipe in `project_ledger.md` §78 if you need to re-verify. Note the app throws its error dialog when the API 404s and the backdrop blocks the nav — strip `.custom-dialog-backdrop` on an interval or nothing is hittable.
- **UNCOMMITTED (2026-08-03) — Stage 31.1, QZ Tray silent printing.** New files: `engines/qz_print.go`, `engines/qz_payload.go`, `engines/qz_print_test.go`, `internal/server/handlers_qz_print.go`, `public/qz-print.js`, `cmd/qzcert/main.go`, `db/migrations_stage31_1_qz_print.sql`, `docs/guides/QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md`. Modified: `internal/server/routes.go`, `public/app.js`, `public/index.html`, plus the three trackers. Full detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 31 and `project_ledger.md` §77. Things worth knowing before touching it:
  - **The migration is NOT applied anywhere.** There is **no PostgreSQL and no Docker running on this machine**, so `migrations_stage31_1_qz_print.sql` has never been executed. Run `erp-server -migrate` before using the feature. Its `DO $$ ... EXECUTE format($f$ ... $f$) ... $$` catch-up block uses **nested dollar-quoting** and has not been parsed by a real Postgres — check it first if the migration run fails.
  - **The signing contract is the fragile part, and it is pinned by tests — do not "simplify" it.** QZ Tray verifies `SHA512withRSA` over the **UTF-8 bytes of the lowercase SHA-256 hex string** of `{"call","params","timestamp"}` (`qz/auth/Certificate.java:363`, `PrintSocketClient.java:218`). Signing the raw digest bytes, or the JSON itself, looks equally correct and fails — **silently**, by making QZ prompt on every label rather than by erroring. `TestQZSignatureIsOverHexStringNotRawDigest` exists specifically to catch that regression. The key order in `public/qz-print.js`'s `JSON.stringify({call, params, timestamp})` is load-bearing too.
  - **`public/qz-print.js` must keep its pure-JS SHA-256.** It is not redundant with `crypto.subtle`: a packing bench reached over plain `http://192.168.x.x` is not a secure context and has no `crypto.subtle` at all. It was fuzz-verified against Node's `crypto` (8 vectors + 300 random lengths).
  - **The sign endpoint only accepts a 64-char hex digest**, deliberately — QZ's own reference backends sign whatever arrives, which is a signing oracle for any authenticated user. Keep the `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` guard if you refactor it.
  - **The signing key auto-generates into `%APPDATA%\custom_erp\qz_private_key.pem`** on first use (or set `QZ_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`/`QZ_CERTIFICATE_PATH`). Regenerating it **invalidates every packing PC's `override.crt`** and printing silently reverts to prompting. Export the public cert with `go run ./cmd/qzcert -o override.crt`.
  - **Nothing has printed on real hardware.** Verified: build/vet/node --check clean, 7 Go tests, and a mock-QZ-Tray end-to-end run (15 assertions, real key material, tampered-signature negative control). Not verified: a real tray, a real printer, the DB-backed endpoints over HTTP. See `micro_checklist.md` 31.1.8 for the precise gap.
  - **`Printer` gained six fields rather than a new table**, so its config screen comes free from the generic Master machinery. Routes share the existing **`stickers`** module key on purpose — a new key 403s every existing tenant (SAAS-0191), the trap 30.7 documented.

Modified: `public/app.js`, `public/index.html`, `docs/guides/USER_GUIDE.md`, `USER_SOP.md`, `UAT_CHECKLIST.md`, `PERMISSION_MATRIX.md` (regenerated). Full detail in `micro_checklist.md` 30.5.5 and `project_ledger.md` §76. Things worth knowing before touching it:

Do not re-add `Stores`; both removal sites in the frontend carry a comment saying so.
  - **If you ever write another migration that removes a doctype, copy this file's structure.** `documents` and `doctype_fields` both `REFERENCES doctype_meta(name) ON DELETE CASCADE` — dropping a `doctype_meta` row **destroys every document of that doctype**, silently. This one moves the data first and guards the `DELETE` on `NOT EXISTS (... WHERE doctype = 'Stores')` so a partial run cannot lose anything.
  - **`documents.id` is unique across all doctypes** (it is the table's PK, not a per-doctype key). That is why the data move is a plain `UPDATE ... SET doctype = 'Location'` with no id remapping — and it is also the long-standing gotcha that a new doctype cannot reuse an id another doctype already holds.
  - **The dev DB had zero `Stores` documents**, so applying the migration there did not exercise the data-move step. It was verified separately against a scratch schema on the real Postgres (15/15, including the cascade guard). If you need to re-verify, rebuild that harness — it was deliberately deleted as one-shot tooling.

Frontend only: `public/app.js`, `public/index.html`, `public/styles.css`, plus the three trackers. No Go, no schema, no migration. Full detail in `micro_checklist.md` 30.5.8 and `project_ledger.md` §75. Things worth knowing before touching it:
  - **`attachLinkTypeahead(el, doctype, opts)` is now the only door bespoke screens use to wire a picker.** `attachTypeahead` is the engine and should not be called directly from a screen any more — the wrapper merges in per-doctype defaults from `TYPEAHEAD_DOCTYPE_OPTS` (currently `Location: {groupBy:'type', showAllOnFocus:true}` and `Employee: {showAllOnFocus:true}`) and then delegates. 37 call sites were converted mechanically. The generic form's Link field and JSONTable link columns route through it too, so a new per-doctype default reaches every screen at once. That table is **presentation only** — do not put schema facts in it; a doctype absent from it just takes the plain default.
  - **Grouping reorders the `items` array, not just the DOM, and this is load-bearing.** `highlight()`/`pick()` address rows by index into `items`, so `.typeahead-group` headings and the `.typeahead-note` footer are deliberately *not* `.typeahead-item`. If you add another non-row element to that menu, keep it out of `.typeahead-item` or arrow-key selection silently picks the wrong record.

  - **The five HR employee `<select>`s are gone**, replaced by one shared `employeePickerField()` + `attachEmployeePicker()`. `employeeOptions()` and `employeePickerHint()` were deleted (zero references). `renderAttendanceTab`/`renderLeaveTab`/`renderPayrollTab`/`renderEmployeeLoansTab` **no longer take an `employees` argument**, and neither `renderHRTab` nor `renderExpensesView` fetches `/api/v1/doc/Employee` up front any more. The stored value is unchanged (`code` is mandatory on Employee, so the typeahead resolves to exactly what `e.code || e.id` sent).
  - **`showAllOnFocus` fires on `focus`, not on click.** Anything scripting these inputs (a UI test, a future autofill) must blur first — clicking an already-focused input will not reopen the menu. This cost two debugging rounds in the verification harness.

  - **If you go looking for more of that defect class, the filter that matters is narrow.** `app.js` has ~352 chained `document.getElementById('x').y` derefs and guarding them wholesale would be pure churn: the normal pattern is *set `container.innerHTML`, then wire the elements you just created*, which cannot be null. Only a deref **after an `await`**, of an element **the function did not itself create**, can hit a detached node. That filter yields 14 sites; 4 (`createASN`) are argument evaluation before the await suspends, 5 are the static login/MFA DOM that lives only in `index.html`, and 2 sit behind a full-viewport modal backdrop. The remaining 3 are the ones fixed above. Re-deriving this costs a session; the scan script was scratch and is gone.

It surfaces as `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`: Go reports *"the system cannot find the file specified"* for a directory that plainly exists, which reads like a bug in your code and is not. PowerShell is trusted and writes fine; writes outside `Documents\` succeed. Any future tool in this repo that generates files needs the same `%TEMP%`-then-copy workaround `docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` uses (or a one-time Defender allowlist entry, which needs admin).

For any setting that also has an env-var override, use `SettingIsOverridden` so an admin edit beats the env var — otherwise the Configuration control renders and saves but does nothing.

- **COMMITTED as `58d6a23` (2026-08-01) — Stage 30.6, server-issued document numbers.** User request from the Purchase Orders screen: *"PO number will be auto created. I will create po sequence. Same of GRN and all other transaction."* 12 doctypes whose create screens used to ask a human to type a number now draw it from the existing `prefix_configs`/`sequence_counters` engine at save time. **New files**: `engines/document_numbering.go` (the doctype → series registry + `PrepareDocumentNumber`), `engines/document_numbering_test.go`, `internal/server/document_numbering_api_test.go`, `db/migrations_stage30_6_auto_document_numbering.sql`. **Modified**: `engines/numbering.go` (`include_store` + a `reset_frequency` that finally does something), `engines/doctype.go` (`FieldMeta.AutoGenerated`), `engines/import.go`, `engines/purchase_requisition_catalog.go`, `internal/server/handlers_core_doc_engine.go`, `public/app.js`, `public/styles.css`. Full detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 30.6 and `project_ledger.md` §70. Things worth knowing before touching it:
  - **The migration is already applied to the dev DB** and recorded in `public.schema_migrations`; a fresh clone needs `erp-server -migrate`. It is additive only — `prefix_configs.include_store` defaults TRUE, so every pre-existing series formats exactly as it did.
  - **Numbers are now document ids.** A generated number becomes `documents.id`, which is unique across *all* doctypes, not per-doctype — so every series prefix must stay distinct. `TestDocumentNumberSeriesKeysAreDistinct` guards this; don't add a registry entry that collides with an existing `prefix_configs.doc_type`.
  - **`reset_frequency` is load-bearing now.** It used to be read and ignored. It now decides both the counter bucket *and* whether the number shows a period segment — deliberately coupled, because a series that resets on a period it doesn't display re-issues last year's numbers, and since the number is the id that is a failed save. Same coupling applies to `include_store`: hiding the store segment also merges the per-store counters.
  - **`ASN.po_number` is a trap.** Despite the name it holds the *referenced PO's* number, not the ASN's own (`asn_number` is that). The registry is per-field because of this; there is a regression test.
  - The generic record form learns which fields are server-issued from `GetDocTypeMeta`'s new `auto_generated` flag, computed from the same registry — so adding a doctype to `documentNumberSeriesByDoctype` updates that form automatically. Don't add a parallel list in JavaScript.

- **~~One test is failing~~ — RESOLVED 2026-08-01 by the auto-numbering session; `go test ./... -p 1` is fully green.** For the trail: a concurrent session correctly observed `TestStatusTransitionMapEnforcedThroughGenericDocAPI` (`internal/server/stage29_8_test.go:113`) failing "Draft->Closed should be rejected 422, status=200", and correctly traced it to the then-in-flight server-side auto-numbering, leaving the call to that work's author rather than guessing. It was the right call, and it surfaced a genuine design error rather than a test problem: `PrepareDocumentNumber` was treating *any* POST to the collection route as a create, but posting a payload `id` to that route is this API's long-standing **upsert** form (`handleGenericDoc` resolves its document id from `payload["id"]` when the path carries none — `ProductContent`'s composite-id save depends on it, and so did that test's fixture). Numbering those turned updates into duplicate documents. Fixed by narrowing the trigger to "a create that names no document": an explicit non-empty `id` is now left alone. The test needed no change. See `micro_checklist.md` Stage 30.6 and `project_ledger.md` §70.

A plain `pg_ctl start` therefore silently lands the *same* database on the wrong port; there was never a second near-empty data dir. Since `db.InitDB` `log.Fatalf`s on a failed connection, one wrong port aborts an entire test-package binary, which is how `go test ./...` became unrunnable. It was restarted on 5435 (`pg_ctl start -D "$env:USERPROFILE\pg-data" -o "-p 5435" -w`), so the defaults in `environments.json`, `manage.ps1`, `routes.go` and all 33 test files are correct again and **no `TEST_DATABASE_URL` override is needed**. Always start it via `./manage.ps1` rather than a bare `pg_ctl start`. (This also settles Stage 30.5.10's claim that `ai_handover.md`'s 5435 was stale — it was not; the environment was.)
  - **⚠️ Migration already applied to the dev DB and recorded in `public.schema_migrations`. Strict mode is LIVE there on 64 doctypes.** If something needs backing out fast, it is one statement: `UPDATE tenant_default.doctype_meta SET strict_status_transitions = FALSE;` — the rules stay, enforcement stops.
  - **⚠️ Nothing is listening on 8080 as of the end of this session** (checked, not assumed — the long-running dev server from earlier sessions is gone). If files you created appear to vanish, check `git stash list` before assuming loss - they are very likely sitting in a named stash, recoverable, and the concurrent session's own pop will very likely restore them; wait for that rather than manually popping a stash another process may be actively managing. - **Stage 26.12.1 — Order Engine (2026-07-24, code)** — the user resolved the design decision left open at the end of the bullet above: a new `SalesOrder`/`SalesOrderLine` doctype pair, not extending `POSCart`. New `engines/orders.go`: `CreateSalesOrder` runs a fixed SKU-mapping → address → payment validate chain (a failing check places the order **On Hold** with a routable `hold_reason` rather than rejecting outright, mirroring `FindBestFulfillmentNode`'s own OMNI-0247 "never hard-reject" precedent one layer earlier), a Hold engine (`PlaceOrderHold`/`ReleaseOrderHold` — release re-runs the *same* validate chain rather than a bespoke resume path), and a stage-gated cancellation matrix (`CancelOrder`, checking the new `StatusTransitionRule` master first and falling back to a hardcoded Shipped/Delivered/Closed/Cancelled blocklist) requiring a mandatory, category-matched `ReasonCode`. New endpoints `POST /api/v1/orders`/`.../{id}/hold`/`.../{id}/release-hold`/`.../{id}/cancel`, `moduleGate("oms",...)`. Deliberately does not rewire existing channel-order webhooks (still create `POSCart` as before) or add a frontend screen yet — both explicit, documented follow-ups, not oversights. **Real gap found+fixed live**: the release-hold endpoint originally always said `"released"` even when the order stayed On Hold; fixed with a new `engines.GetOrderStatus` so the response reflects what actually happened. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean (new `OrderEngine` subtest); live-verified over real HTTP (throwaway port 8213) including a full create→hold→release→cancel lifecycle and `moduleGate` enforcement. Full detail: `project_ledger.md` §47.
- **Stage 26.12.2 — Allocation/Sourcing Engine (2026-07-25, code)** — new `engines.ResolveAllocationPlan` (`engines/sourcing.go`) runs the tenant's configured `AllocationRule` rows (26.12.9) in priority order, trying five strategies beyond the pre-existing Highest-ATS default: Nearest Pincode (needed a new additive `Location.pincode` field, `db/migrations_stage26_12_2_allocation_sourcing.sql`), Lowest Workload (open `FulfillmentTask` count), Oldest Stock (`inventory_availability.updated_at` as a documented proxy), Split Shipment (the only strategy allowed to spread lines across multiple locations), and Manual (always routes to a human, short-circuits the search). Falls back to the exact pre-26.12.2 behavior when no rules are configured; once rules exist, exhausting all of them without a plan holds the order with a new `HoldAllocationFailed`/`ALLOCATION_FAILED` code instead of a new "exception queue" table — the On-Hold `SalesOrder` rows filtered by `hold_reason` already are that queue. `CreateSalesOrder`/`ReleaseOrderHold` reworked to reserve per line (`plan.LineLocations[i]`) instead of one shared location per order, since the schema already supported this. Reallocation-on-reject (`fulfillment.go`) deliberately left untouched — its existing single-transaction boundary already gives it the rollback-on-failure property the design note asks for. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean (new `AllocationSourcing` subtest covering all 5 non-default strategies + the exception path); live-verified over real HTTP (throwaway port 8237) — a real order routed to the nearer of two seeded locations by pincode, and a second real order on a Manual-ruled channel went On Hold with `ALLOCATION_FAILED` despite being fully in stock. All fixtures cleaned up after. Full detail: `project_ledger.md` §48.
- **Stage 26.12.3 — Fulfillment Pick/Pack (2026-07-25, code, UNCOMMITTED as of this note)** — new `engines/fulfillment_pickpack.go` (a separate file from `fulfillment.go`'s existing `CreateFulfillmentTasks`/`TransitionTaskStatus`, both untouched) layers scan-validated Pick and Pack stages on top of `FulfillmentTask.items` via three new additive per-line JSON fields (`picked_qty`/`packed_qty`/`short_qty`). `ScanPickItem`/`ScanPackItem` resolve a scanned barcode (falling back to SKU/code, same convention as the 26.3.1 GRN barcode preview) against the first still-short task line for that SKU, naming the actual product on a mismatch rather than a bare "invalid scan"; the hard rule that packed qty can never exceed picked qty falls directly out of `ScanPackItem`'s own match condition rather than a separate check. `ShortPickLine` marks a line's whole remaining shortfall short in one action, gated on a mandatory `ReasonCode` (reused the exact 26.12.1 Hold/Cancel convention; needed one small migration adding a "Short Pick" category value, `db/migrations_stage26_12_3_pick_pack.sql`). `CompletePackTask` is the explicit, blockable completion action — hard-blocks unless every line both fully resolved its pick and packed exactly what was picked. Deliberately cross-checked `engines/wms.go` first per the design note and kept it separate rather than merged: `GenerateBinPickList` answers "which bin to walk to," this answers "did the picker/packer get the right units" — orthogonal concerns. Deliberately skipped auto-generating an invoice on pack completion (no Order→Shipment→Invoice wiring exists yet to generate one from). New endpoints (`internal/server/handlers_wms.go`, `routes.go`): `POST /api/v1/wms/pick-scan`/`pack-scan`/`short-pick`/`pack-complete`, `moduleGate("wms",...)`. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean (new `PickPack` subtest, 14 assertions across one fixture); live-verified over real HTTP (throwaway port 8241) — full pick→short-pick→pack→complete lifecycle through the real endpoints, final document state confirmed exactly (`picked_qty=2`/`short_qty=1`/`packed_qty=2`/`status=Packed`). All fixtures cleaned up after. Full detail: `project_ledger.md` §49. **Next**: per the sprint's recommended order, `26.12.5` (Returns/RTO/QC/Refund) — `26.12.4` (Courier/Shipment/Manifest) after that.

Bonus bug fixed: the generic Link-field `<select>` was building its value from the wrong record field. **24.36**: new best-effort offline-queue heartbeat (`POST /api/v1/pos/offline-heartbeat`) + server-side gap detection at session-close (`POSOfflineQueueGap`, new sidebar screen) — closes the "always untraceable" case down to "untraceable only with zero connectivity ever between queueing and wiping," stated honestly as a residual limit, not oversold as fully solved. **20.6**: ran the scale simulator well past its checked-in test scale (800 tx/50 workers, then a same-location contention variant) — 0 failures either way; messy-data testing found and fixed 2 real things: this dev Postgres cluster is WIN1252-encoded not UTF8 (any CJK/Arabic/emoji text hard-fails on save) — added `db.EnforceUTF8Encoding()` (warns in dev, refuses to boot in `ENV=production`) rather than destructively recreating the shared dev DB; and unexpected 500s were leaking raw internal error text to API clients — fixed at the one `writeAPIErrorGeneric` choke point. - **`docs/ERP_LOOPHOLES_ANALYSIS.md` reconciliation + a live maker-checker bypass closed (2026-07-27, docs + code, UNCOMMITTED as of this note)** — user asked to bring the loophole doc itself up to date; §129's 2026-07-24 addendum and §60's 24.33/24.34 fixes had already re-verified/closed 19 of its 21 "still open" items, but nobody had rewritten the doc itself to say so — it still read "21 open" to anyone opening it directly. Rewrote it end-to-end with a fresh, independent file/function citation per item (not just trusting the prior audits' prose): 19 moved to a new "Verified Fixed/Mitigated" section, Medium #12 (CSRF) reclassified **Not Applicable** (verified via grep for `http.Cookie`/`SetCookie` — zero hits, this app is Bearer-token/localStorage-only). One small code change: `requireHRAdmin` (`internal/server/handlers_admin_identity.go`) now explicitly rejects `Resolved-Purpose == "extension"` — belt-and-suspenders behind all 17 call sites, even though no live bypass existed. Two near-misses caught and reverted before landing: an almost-duplicate DB-pool-stats endpoint (24.34 already put `db_pool` on `handleHealth`) and an almost-"tightened" `poData` unmarshal in `transactional_validation.go` that §60's own 24.33 note had already explicitly reviewed and left as low-risk-by-design — reverted both to respect the existing record rather than re-litigate from a cold read. **User then asked to fix the one item left open (Medium #9, status transitions) too, rather than leave it as a decision to defer.** Digging past this doc's own prior framing found the real risk was a live, actively-exploitable bug, not a cosmetic gap: `handleGenericDoc`'s generic doc create/update path took `payload["status"]` with zero restriction — any user with plain create/update rights on an approval-gated doctype (PurchaseOrder, VendorInvoice, JournalVoucher, LoyaltyRedemptionRequest, ...) could set `"status": "Approved"` directly, fully bypassing `SubmitForApproval`/`DecideApproval` (no role check, no maker-checker segregation-of-duties check). Closed the decision-free half of the finding: `handleGenericDoc` now rejects (`GLOBAL-0019`) a client-supplied `Approved`/`Rejected` status that differs from the document's prior status whenever the doctype is approval-gated — enforcing the approval engine's own already-defined states, not a new business rule. `statusVal != priorStatus` deliberately lets an already-`Approved` document's unchanged-status round-trip edit pass through exactly as before (the existing `wasApproved`/`ResetToPendingOnEdit` reset logic is untouched). What remains genuinely open, narrower now: a general per-doctype transition map for non-approval-gated doctypes (Masters' Active/Inactive, GRN/ProductionOrder's own flows) — a real scope/UX decision, not a security gap. Live-verified against two scratch instances (ports 8199 and 8299, real `admin`/`manager1` seeded users): extension token → 403 on `POST /api/v1/prefix`; direct create/edit-to-`Approved` → 422 `GLOBAL-0019`; normal `Draft` create/edit → 200; full legitimate submit→approve by a second real user → 200; an already-`Approved` PO edited with status round-tripped unchanged → 200, correctly reset to `Pending Approval` afterward. `go build`/`go vet` clean throughout; `go test ./... -p 1` clean in `internal/server`, one pre-existing unrelated failure in `engines` (`TestReportsStage26_10/ScheduledReportWorker`, a date-rollover edge case in a concurrent session's own in-flight `reports_stage26_10.go` edit, already sitting in the tree before this session started). All scratch fixtures/tooling removed after. Full detail: `project_ledger.md` §61.

New doctypes throughout deliberately followed one of two existing patterns only: the `documents`-table-log pattern (`StockLedgerEntry`/`ReportRunLog` precedent) for anything system-written, or the flat-doctype-plus-existing-approval-engine pattern (`QualityInspection`/`PurchaseRequisition` precedent) for anything a human submits — no new bespoke table, no parallel approval mechanism. Two real pre-existing bugs found and fixed along the way: `advanceScheduleDate` (Stage 26.10.4) compared a parsed date's UTC-midnight against `time.Now()`'s local time-of-day (could misjudge a future date as "not past today" depending on server timezone) — this is the exact failure §149's own entry above logged as "a concurrent session's in-flight edit, not investigated further"; it's now fixed. And the generic approval-submit endpoint needs `allow_update`, not `allow_create` — Grievance's first migration draft would have silently blocked every employee's own self-service submission. New files: `engines/{crm_analytics,manufacturing_scheduling,wms_slotting,wms_productivity,wms_3pl_billing,wms_robotics}.go`, `internal/server/{handlers_crm_analytics,handlers_wms_p2}.go`, 5 migration files, plus additive edits to `engines/report_registry.go`/`scheduled_reports.go`/`wms.go`/`transfer_orders.go` and `public/app.js`/`index.html` (Customers screen, Mobile Picking screen, 5 new tabs across Manufacturing/HR). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean (4 new test files, real Postgres). - **Go-live decisions/dormant-Docker work committed (2026-07-28, commit `b980491`)** — user asked to commit specifically the go-live-decisions-doc + dormant-container-prep work (§146/§148 above): `docs/operations/go_live_decisions.md`, `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `docker-compose.yml`, and the `docker-build-check` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. `git status`/`git diff` review first (per this file's own shared-tree convention) found §146/§148's own doc edits to `docs/ai_handover.md`/`docs/micro_checklist.md` had already been swept into an earlier concurrent-session commit (their content confirmed present in HEAD before touching either file again — so neither was re-staged here, avoiding a duplicate/conflicting edit), and that `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s diff was clean (only the new job). **A live git-hygiene incident happened staging even this small, deliberately-scoped set**: `git add` was run against only those 5 files, confirmed via `git status --porcelain` immediately after showing exactly those 5 staged — but by the time `git commit` ran moments later, a concurrent session's own broad stage/commit activity had added far more to the same index, and the resulting commit `b980491` carries 35 files (the concurrent session's own already-working §150 P2-bundle files, `docs/project_ledger.md`, `engines/engines_test.go`, etc.), not the 5 intended. Per this repo's established precedent for this exact failure mode (§51/§52/§57/§58's own notes: never rewrite history to fix it, since a concurrently-active tree makes `reset`/`amend` its own hazard) — left as-is rather than amended. Not data loss: every extra file was the other session's own real, already-tested work that needed committing anyway, just filed under this commit's message instead of its own. `go build`/`go vet` re-run clean against the resulting HEAD to confirm nothing broke. Full backlog/scope-per-item and the code changes themselves are unaffected by which commit message they ended up under.

Also included: `.gitattributes` pinning LF on `deploy/` (bash/systemd/Caddy files break on a CRLF Windows checkout), and `TEMP_menu_naming_comparison.md`, a 2026-07-23 scratch doc self-marked "delete once decided" — committed because the ask was "commit all". **Since deleted** (2026-07-29, commit `e2ba733`) at the user's explicit go-ahead during a cleanup pass; the naming call itself was still open at that point, so recover the comparison table from `a3b4c16` if it's needed.

- **Standing verification practice** (true for nearly every entry below, not repeated each time): built on a throwaway port, `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` run clean, live-verified against the real dev DB, test data cleaned up afterward. Only deviations (skipped verification, a failed test, left-over data) are called out per entry.

## Current State (check before trusting anything above)

- **Git**: this section's commit hash goes stale fast in a tree with this much concurrent-session activity — trust §6's "Latest commit" bullet and `git log --oneline -10` over any specific hash written here. As of this note, this session's own four commits (Phase 26.3 close-out, Stage 26.12 foundation/Order-Engine/Allocation-Sourcing, the `OrderEngine`/`AllocationSourcing` test-coverage catch-up, and 26.12.4 Courier/Shipment/Manifest) are in; a concurrent session's 26.12.3 and apparent Stage 26.5 work is not yet committed. Run `git status --porcelain` to confirm current state before trusting anything else in this file.
- **Unrelated in-flight work**: `docs/Contract/`, `docs/extension_hooks_checklist.md`, `docs/github_checklist.md` — a separate session's legal/security planning docs, nothing to do with the Stage tracker. Don't sweep these into an unrelated commit. (`TEMP_menu_naming_comparison.md` was on this list until 2026-07-29; it's now deleted — see the binaries bullet below.)
- **Local binaries**: only `erp-server.exe` remains (built 2026-07-27) — the one `manage.ps1` starts. It predates every commit from Stage 28 onward, so rebuild before assuming the live app reflects recent work. The stale `custom_erp.exe`/`erp-pim16-final.exe`/`server.exe` and the 2026-07-12 `public/dist/` bundle were deleted 2026-07-29 as unreferenced build artifacts (all gitignored; `public/dist/` regenerates with `npm run build` and is not referenced by `index.html`). `TEMP_menu_naming_comparison.md` was deleted in the same pass at the user's explicit go-ahead (commit `e2ba733`); recover it from `a3b4c16` if the naming comparison is wanted again.
- **Concurrent sessions are common in this repo** — this tree has repeatedly seen 2-3 sessions (and the user) editing it in the same window. Before any edit or commit:
  1. Run `git status`/`git diff` first — never assume the tree is clean.
  2. Never `git add -A` — stage only the files you actually reviewed.
  3. If a build fails transiently mid-edit, it may be another session's in-flight change — wait and retry before debugging.
  - Past collisions (for pattern recognition, not action needed): a Stage-numbering clash (20 vs 21) caught via `git status` and renumbered before shipping; three sessions editing the same files in one window on 2026-07-20; two sessions independently picking the identical Stage 25 batch on 2026-07-23 and converging on the same fix.
- **Known issues**: `docs/operations/hardening_roadmap.md` (security/correctness backlog) fully closed as of 2026-07-12, historical record only. `docs/specs/pdf_blueprint_gap_analysis.md` is a superseded 2026-07-12 snapshot. Active backlog lives in `docs/micro_checklist.md`.
- **Test suite**: fully green as of Stage 26.0.2 (2026-07-24) — the two long-standing failures this bullet used to document (`FinanceDoubleEntryAndPOS`'s stale trial-balance assertion, `DocTypeValidationAndAuth`'s shared-DB `Brand.fefo_enabled` debris) were both root-caused and fixed, not worked around. Re-confirmed clean again 2026-07-25 after the Stage 26.12.2 Allocation/Sourcing work (`go test ./... -p 1`, whole suite, incl. a new `AllocationSourcing` subtest). **2026-07-27 note**: `internal/server` still clean; `engines` now shows one new failure, `TestReportsStage26_10/ScheduledReportWorker` (a today's-date rollover edge case) — traced to a concurrent session's own in-flight, uncommitted edit to `engines/reports_stage26_10.go` (already modified in the tree before the loophole-doc reconciliation session above started), not a regression from that session's own changes. Worth a fresh look once that concurrent edit lands/settles.
- Every test file's `db.InitDB` uses a hardcoded connection string to the real `custom_erp` DB on port 5435 — `DATABASE_URL` has no effect on `go test`. Apply new migrations to `custom_erp`, not just `custom_erp_test`.

## 7. Handover Notes for Incoming AI (Claude / Codex / Gemini)

Welcome! The core system plus a growing set of business modules (POS, Finance/GL, GST, MFA, approvals, HR, Fixed Assets, Expenses, CRM/Loyalty, Manufacturing) are built and operational.

Before treating any `[x]` checklist item as fully closed, read its entry in `docs/micro_checklist.md` — each one documents its exact scope and any deliberate limitations.

**To resume development:**
1. Pull latest `main`.
2. Start PostgreSQL on port `5435`.
3. Run `go test ./...` to verify business rules.
4. Start the server: `./erp-server.exe` or `go run ./cmd/server`.
5. Open `http://localhost:8080` — log in with `DEV_CREDENTIALS.local.txt` (gitignored, project root; regenerate via a throwaway bcrypt script if missing, then update `db/migration.sql` and the live `users` table).
6. See `docs/project_ledger.md` for the full chronological build history.
